  ajax-after="1343801-1344389.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.1-20210604.html "Link to previous version of section 5.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893504 .Subheading}

**[[5.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a heating boiler that has a wetted heating surface of
  3 m^2^ or less;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has a capacity of 40 L or
  less;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that is installed for use at a
  pressure of one atmosphere of pressure or less;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  152 mm or less;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  610 mm or less and that is used to store hot water;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  610 mm or less connected to a water pumping system and that contains
  compressed air which serves as a cushion;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a hydropneumatic tank that has an internal diameter
  of 610 mm or less;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} an interprovincial or international pipeline; or

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} a refrigeration plant that has a capacity of 18 kW or
  less.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

### [Fabrication and Installation Standards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893517 .Subheading}

**[[5.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.3 .sectionLabel}** Every boiler, pressure
vessel and pressure piping system used in a work place shall, if
feasible, meet the standards relating to design, construction, testing,
stamping, nameplates, fabrication inspection and installation set out in
clauses 4.15 to 4.18 and 5.1 to 9.1 of Part I of the Boiler Code.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 11
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 23](#1177362-1188002){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177362-1188002.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.3"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[5.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every boiler and pressure vessel
  shall have at least one safety valve or other equivalent fitting to
  maintain pressure at or below the maximum allowable working pressure
  of the boiler or pressure vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} When two or more boilers or pressure vessels are
  connected and are used at a common operating pressure, they shall each
  be fitted with at least one safety valve or other equivalent fitting
  to maintain pressure at or below the maximum allowable working
  pressure of the boiler or pressure vessel that has the lowest maximum
  allowable working pressure.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

### [Use, Operation, Repair, Alteration and Maintenance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893526 .Subheading}

- **[[5.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer shall ensure that a qualified person, charged with the
  operation of a boiler, is in attendance and readily available at all
  times while the boiler is in operation and other employees are
  normally present in the same building as a boiler.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The attendance referred to in subsection (1) is not
  required if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the boiler is equipped with a fail-safe device and
    an automated warning device that will ensure the safe operation of
    the boiler and its shutdown if required, and that are installed in
    such a manner that they

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} cannot be rendered inoperative, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} can be tested under operating conditions; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the boiler is rated below 2,000 kW in the case of a
    high pressure boiler, and below 3,000 kW in the case of a low
    pressure boiler, which includes a low pressure hot water boiler, a
    low pressure organic fluid boiler and a low pressure steam boiler.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} When a boiler is shut down by a device referred to in
  paragraph (2)(a), the boiler shall not be restarted unless it has been
  examined by a qualified person and the cause of the shutdown
  rectified.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 10(F)
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
- SOR/2014-142, s. 3(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.5"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[5.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.6 .sectionLabel}** Every boiler, pressure
vessel and pressure piping system in use at a work place shall be
operated, maintained and repaired only by a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

**[[5.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.7 .sectionLabel}** All repairs and welding
of boilers, pressure vessels and pressure piping systems shall be
carried out in accordance with the standards referred to in clauses 6.1,
7.1 and 8.1 of Part I of the Boiler Code.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

**[[5.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.8 .sectionLabel}** No person shall alter,
interfere with or render inoperative any fitting attached to a boiler or
pressure vessel except for the purpose of adjusting or testing the
fitting.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
- SOR/2009-147, s. 6(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.8"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[5.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  factor of safety of a high pressure lap-seam riveted boiler, shall be
  increased by at least 0.1 each year after 20 years of use.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the boiler is relocated at any time, it shall not
  be operated at a pressure that is more than one atmosphere of
  pressure.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

### [Inspections]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893551 .Subheading}

- **[[5.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall use a boiler, pressure vessel or pressure piping system
  unless it has been inspected by an inspector in accordance with
  subsection (2) and it has been certified by the inspector as safe for
  its intended use as stated in the declaration referred to in
  subparagraph 5.18(2)(b)(v).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The inspector shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} inspect every boiler, pressure vessel and pressure
    piping system

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} after it is installed and before it is used for
      the first time,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} after any welding, alteration or repair is
      carried out on it, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with section 5.11, 5.12 or 5.16;
      and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make a record of each inspection in accordance with
    section 5.18.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who operates, repairs or maintains a boiler,
  pressure vessel or pressure piping system or any part of it may not
  inspect the boiler, pressure vessel or pressure piping system for the
  purposes of subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

- **[[5.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every high pressure boiler and every
  low pressure steam boiler in use at a work place shall be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once each year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally, at least once every two years.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every low pressure hot water boiler and every unfired
  boiler in use at a work place shall be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once every two years; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally, at least once every four years.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every low pressure organic fluid boiler in use at a
  work place shall be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once every two years; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally, at least once every three years.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every waste heat boiler in use at a work place shall
  be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once every year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at least once every two years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where ultrasonic thickness measurements are
      performed annually by an NDT technician, at least once every three
      years.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Pressure vessels, other than buried pressure vessels,
  that have a corrosion rate exceeding 0.1 mm of metal loss per year
  shall be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once every year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at least once every two years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if ultrasonic thickness measurements are
      performed annually by an NDT technician on representative sections
      of the pressure vessel, at least once every three years.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Pressure vessels, other than buried pressure vessels,
  that have a corrosion not exceeding 0.1 mm of metal loss per year
  shall be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once every year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at least once every four years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if ultrasonic thickness measurements are
      performed annually by an NDT technician on representative sections
      of the pressure vessel, at least once every six years.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Air receivers shall be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once every year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally, at least once every five years.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} If the known corrosion rate of a pressure vessel is
  zero, internal inspection is not necessary provided that complete
  external inspections, including nondestructive thickness measurements
  performed by an NDT technician, are made at least once every two years
  on the pressure vessel and the following conditions are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the non-corrosive nature of the service conditions,
    including the effect of trace components, has been established by at
    least five continuous years of comparable service experience with
    the fluid being handled;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the periodic external inspection indicates that the
    condition of the pressure vessel does not warrant any further
    investigation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operating temperature and pressure of the
    pressure vessel does not exceed the lower limits for the creep
    rupture range of the vessel metal; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the pressure vessel is protected against
    inadvertent contamination, and there is no evidence of
    contamination.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} If a pressure vessel is used to store anhydrous
  ammonia, the internal inspection frequency referred to in paragraph
  (5)(b) may be replaced by an internal inspection conducted at least
  once every five years if, at the same time, a hydrostatic test at a
  pressure equal to one and one-half times the maximum allowable working
  pressure is conducted.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
- SOR/2014-142, s. 4(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.11"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[5.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.12 .sectionLabel}** Every boiler,
pressure vessel and pressure piping system in use at a work place shall
be inspected by an inspector more frequently than it is provided for in
section 5.11, if it is necessary to ensure that the boiler, pressure
vessel or pressure piping system is safe for its intended use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 11
- SOR/94-263, s. 12
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

**[[5.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.13 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsections 5.11(5), (6) and (8), the corrosion rate shall be determined
from actual metal loss during the last appropriate inspection period
specified in the subsections.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 12(F)
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

**[[5.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.14 .sectionLabel}** Stationary or
portable air receivers used in the railway industry, instead of being
inspected in accordance with subsection 5.11(7) and a record completed
in accordance with section 5.18, may be inspected and tested and a
record filed in accordance with TC-0-010, Rules for the Installation,
Inspection and Testing of Air Reservoirs (Other Than on Motive Power
Equipment) made by the Railway Association of Canada and approved by the
Minister of Transport on December 5, 1994.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 13(F)
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

**[[5.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.15 .sectionLabel}** If a pressure vessel
that contains materials hazardous to human health or the environment is
to be emptied before being inspected, it shall be emptied and inspected
in a manner that does not pose a risk to human health or the
environment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
:::

### [Buried Pressure Vessels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893611 .Subheading}

- **[[5.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The installation of a buried
  pressure vessel shall meet the requirements set out in Appendix A to
  Part I of the Boiler Code.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of the proposed backfilling shall be given to
  the Head of Compliance and Enforcement before backfilling is done over
  a pressure vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If test plates are used as an indication of corrosion
  of a buried pressure vessel, the test plates and, subject to
  subsection (4), the pressure vessel shall be completely uncovered and
  inspected by an inspector at least once every three years.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, on an inspection referred to in subsection (3),
  the test plates show no corrosion, the buried pressure vessel may be
  completely uncovered and inspected at intervals exceeding three years
  if the employer, immediately after the inspection, notifies the Head
  of Compliance and Enforcement in writing of the condition of the test
  plates and of the proposed inspection schedule for the pressure
  vessel.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every buried pressure vessel shall be completely
  uncovered and inspected at least once every 15 years.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 15(F)
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
- SOR/2009-147, s. 7(F)
- SOR/2014-148, s. 3
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312240){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312240.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.16-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 5.16"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Records and Reports]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893620 .Subheading}

**[[5.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.17 .sectionLabel}** The employer shall
keep a record of every boiler, pressure vessel and pressure piping
system to which this Part applies and that is under the employer's
control.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 16(F)
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 24(E)](#1177364-1188004){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177364-1188004.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.17"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[5.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  report of each inspection carried out under sections 5.10 to 5.16
  shall be completed by the inspector who carried out the inspection.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every report referred to in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be signed by the inspector who carried out
    the inspection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall include

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of the inspection,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the identification and location of the boiler,
      pressure vessel or pressure piping system that was inspected,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the maximum allowable working pressure and the
      maximum temperature at which the boiler or pressure vessel may be
      operated,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a declaration as to whether the boiler, pressure
      vessel or pressure piping system meets the standards prescribed by
      this Part,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a declaration as to whether the boiler, pressure
      vessel or pressure piping system is safe for its intended use,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a list of any defects or deficiencies the
      inspector has observed in the condition or operating and
      maintenance practices of the boiler, pressure vessel or pressure
      piping system, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} any other observation that the inspector
      considers relevant to the safety of employees.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep readily available every
  record of inspection for the last two inspection periods and on the
  request of the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative, shall provide the work place committee or the health
  and safety representative with a copy.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2001-284, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 25(F)](#1177366-1188010){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177366-1188010.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.18-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.18"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART VI]{.HLabel1}[Lighting]{.HTitleText1} {#h-893639 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893640 .Subheading}

- **[[6.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Part,

  [[aerodrome apron]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aerodrome apron]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means that part of a land
      aerodrome intended to accommodate the loading and unloading of
      passengers and cargo and the refuelling, servicing, maintenance
      and parking of aircraft; ([aire de trafic]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[aircraft stand]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aircraft stand]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means that part of an
      aerodrome apron intended to be used for the parking of aircraft
      for the purpose of loading or unloading passengers and providing
      ground services; ([poste de stationnement]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[primary grain elevator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[primary grain elevator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a grain
      elevator the principal use of which is the receiving of grain
      directly from producers for storage or forwarding; ([installation
      primaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[task position]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[task position]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a position at which a
      visual task is performed; ([poste de travail]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[VDT]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[VDT]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a visual display terminal.
      ([TEV]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part, 1 lx is equal to .0929
  fc.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893651 .Subheading}

**[[6.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply in respect of lighting in any underground portion of a coal mine.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Measurement of Average Levels of Lighting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893655 .Subheading}

**[[6.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.3 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 6.4 to 6.10, the average level of lighting at a task position
or in an area shall be determined

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} by making four measurements at different places
  representative of the level of lighting at the task position or, in an
  area, representative of the level of lighting 1 m above the floor of
  the area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} by dividing the aggregate of the results of those
  measurements by four.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Lighting --- Office Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893661 .Subheading}

**[[6.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.4 .sectionLabel}** The average level of
lighting at a task position or in an area set out in Column I of an item
of Schedule I, other than a task position or area referred to in section
6.7 or 6.9, shall not be less than the level set out in Column II of
that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Lighting --- Industrial Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893665 .Subheading}

**[[6.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.5 .sectionLabel}** The average level of
lighting in an area set out in Column I of an item of Schedule II, other
than an area referred to in section 6.7 or 6.9, shall not be less than
the level set out in Column II of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Lighting --- General Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893669 .Subheading}

**[[6.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.6 .sectionLabel}** The average level of
lighting in an area set out in Column I of an item of Schedule III,
other than an area referred to in section 6.7 or 6.9, shall not be less
than the level set out in Column II of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Lighting --- VDT]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893673 .Subheading}

- **[[6.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  average level of lighting at a task position or in an area set out in
  Column I of an item of Schedule IV shall not be more than the level
  set out in Column II of that item.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Reflection glare on a VDT screen shall be reduced to
  the point where an employee at a task position is able to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} read every portion of any text displayed on the
    screen; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} see every portion of the visual display on the
    screen.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where VDT work requires the reading of a document,
  supplementary lighting shall be provided where necessary to give a
  level of lighting of at least 500 lx on the document.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Lighting --- Aerodrome Aprons and Aircraft Stands]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893682 .Subheading}

- **[[6.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  average level of lighting at a task position on an aerodrome apron
  shall not be less than 10 lx.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The average level of lighting at a task position on
  an aircraft stand shall not be less than 20 lx.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Lighting --- Artefactual Exhibits and Archival Materials]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893688 .Subheading}

**[[6.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.9 .sectionLabel}** The average level of
lighting in an area in which artefactual exhibits or archival materials
are handled or stored shall not be less than 50 lx.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Emergency Lighting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893692 .Subheading}

- **[[6.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Emergency lighting shall be provided
  to illuminate the following areas within buildings:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exits and corridors;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} principal routes providing access to exits in open
    floor areas; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} floor areas where employees normally congregate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except in the case of a primary grain elevator in
  which hand-held lamps are used for emergency lighting, all emergency
  lighting provided in accordance with subsection (1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} operate automatically in the event that the regular
    power supply to the building is interrupted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide an average level of lighting of not less
    than 10 lx; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be independent of the regular power source.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a generator is used as a power source for
  emergency lighting, the inspection, testing and maintenance of the
  generator shall be in accordance with the requirements referred to in
  section 6.7 of the National Fire Code, as amended from time to time.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a central storage battery system is used as a
  power source for emergency lighting or where emergency lighting is
  provided by a self-contained emergency lighting unit, the battery
  system or the unit shall be tested

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} monthly by hand; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} annually under simulated power failure or
    electrical fault conditions.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a battery, other than a hermetically sealed
  battery, is tested in accordance with paragraph (4)(a), the
  electrolyte level of the battery shall be checked and, if necessary,
  adjusted to the proper level.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where a self-contained emergency lighting unit is
  tested in accordance with paragraph (4)(b), all lamps forming part of
  the unit shall be operated for the time period set out in Sentence
  3.2.7.3(2) of the National Building Code, as amended from time to
  time, that is applicable to the class of buildings to which the
  building in which the unit is installed belongs.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall make a record of the results of
  each test performed in accordance with subsection (3) or (4) and keep
  the record for two years after the test.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

### [Minimum Levels of Lighting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893711 .Subheading}

- **[[6.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (4),
  the level of lighting at any place at a task position or in an area
  that may be measured for the purposes of section 6.3 shall not be less
  than one third of the level of lighting prescribed by this Part for
  that task position or area.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The level of lighting at any place at a task position
  or in an area set out in Column I of item 8 or 9 of Schedule III or
  Column I of item 1 of Schedule IV that may be measured for the
  purposes of section 6.3 shall not be less than one tenth of the level
  of lighting prescribed by this Part for that task position or area.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The level of lighting at any place at a task position
  referred to in section 6.8 that may be measured for the purposes of
  section 6.3 shall not be less than one quarter of the level of
  lighting prescribed by this Part for that task position.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} In a building the construction of which is commenced
  after October 31, 1990, the level of emergency lighting at any place
  in an area referred to in subsection 6.10(1) that may be measured for
  the purposes of section 6.3 shall not be less than 0.25 lx.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-515, s. 1
:::

:::::: section
::::: {#893719 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE I]{.scheduleLabel}[(Section 6.4)]{.OriginatingRef} {#h-893720 .scheduleLabel}
:::

<figure id="893721">

## [PART VII]{.HLabel1}[Levels of Sound]{.HTitleText1} {#h-893759 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893760 .Subheading}

**[[7.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[A-weighted sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[A-weighted sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    sound pressure level as determined by a measurement system which
    includes an A-weighting filter that meets the requirements set out
    in the International Electrotechnical Commission Standard 651
    (1979), Sound Level Meters, as amended from time to time; ([niveau
    de pression acoustique pondérée A]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dBA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dBA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means decibel A-weighted and is a unit
    of A-weighted sound pressure level; ([dBA]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[large truck]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[large truck]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, SOR/98-589, s.
    1\]]{.Repealed}

[[noise exposure level (L~ex,8~)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[noise exposure level (L~ex,8~)]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means 10
    times the logarithm to the base 10 of the time integral over any 24
    hour period of a squared A-weighted sound pressure divided by 8, the
    reference sound pressure being 20 µPa; ([niveau d'exposition
    (L~ex,8~)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[sound level meter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sound level meter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device for
    measuring sound pressure level that meets the performance
    requirements for a Type 2 instrument as specified in the
    International Electrotechnical Commission Standard 651 (1979), Sound
    Level Meters, as amended from time to time;
    ([sonomètre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sound pressure level]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means 20 times the
    logarithm to the base 10 of the ratio of the root mean square
    pressure of a sound to the reference sound pressure of 20 µPa,
    expressed in decibels. ([niveau de pression
    acoustique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/98-589, s. 1
:::

### [Measurement and Calculation of Exposure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893770 .Subheading}

- **[[7.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this Part, the exposure of an employee to sound shall
  be measured using an instrument that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is recommended for that measurement in clause 4.3
    of CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z107.56-13, Measurement of Noise Exposure;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements for such an instrument set
    out in clause 4 of the Standard referred to in paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The exposure of an employee to sound shall be
  measured in accordance with clauses 5, 6.4.1, 6.4.4, 6.5.2, 6.5.4,
  6.6.2 and 6.6.4 of the Standard referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part, the measurement and
  calculation of the noise exposure level (L~ex,\ 8~) to which an
  employee is exposed shall take into account the exposure of the
  employee to A-weighted sound pressure levels of 74 dBA and greater.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The measurement and calculation of the noise exposure
  level (L~ex,\ 8~) referred to in subsection (3) may also take into
  account the exposure of the employee to A-weighted sound pressure
  levels that are less than 74 dBA.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/2018-247, s. 1
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344390){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344390.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.2-20181123.html "Link to previous version of section 7.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Hazard Investigation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893780 .Subheading}

- **[[7.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee in a work place
  may be exposed to an A-weighted sound pressure level equal to or
  greater than 84 dBA for a duration that is likely to endanger the
  employee's hearing, the employer shall, without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appoint a qualified person to carry out an
    investigation of the degree of exposure; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify the work place committee or the health and
    safety representative of the investigation and the name of the
    person appointed to carry out the investigation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/98-589, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the measurement
  of the A-weighted sound pressure level in a work place shall be
  performed instantaneously, during normal working conditions, using the
  slow response setting of a sound level meter.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the investigation referred to in subsection (1),
  the following matters shall be considered:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the sources of sound in the work place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the A-weighted sound pressure levels to which the
    employee is likely to be exposed and the duration of such exposure;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the methods being used to reduce this exposure;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether the exposure of the employee is likely to
    exceed the limits prescribed by section 7.4; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the employee is likely to be exposed to a
    noise exposure level (L~ex,8~) equal to or greater than 84 dBA.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the investigation and after
  consultation with the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative, the person appointed to carry out the investigation
  shall set out in a written report signed and dated by the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} observations respecting the matters considered in
    accordance with subsection (4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} recommendations respecting the measures that should
    be taken in order to comply with sections 7.4 to 7.8; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} recommendations respecting the use of hearing
    protectors by employees who are exposed to a noise exposure level
    (L~ex,8~) equal to or greater than 84 dBA and not greater than 87
    dBA.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The report shall be kept by the employer at the work
  place in respect of which it applies for a period of ten years after
  the date of the report.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where it is stated in the report that an employee is
  likely to be exposed to a noise exposure level (L~ex,8~) equal to or
  greater than 84 dBA, the employer shall, without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} post and keep posted a copy of the report in a
    conspicuous place in the work place in respect of which it applies;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide the employee with written information
    describing the hazards associated with exposure to high levels of
    sound.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/98-589, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 10
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 26(F)](#1177368-1188011){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177368-1188011.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 7.3"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Limits of Exposure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893803 .Subheading}

**[[7.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.4 .sectionLabel}** No employee in a work
place shall, in any 24 hour period, be exposed to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an A-weighted sound pressure level set out in column
  I of the schedule for a duration of exposure exceeding the applicable
  duration set out in column II, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a noise exposure level (L~ex\ 8~) that exceeds 87
  dBA.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/98-589, s. 3
:::

### [Reduction of Sound Exposure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893809 .Subheading}

**[[7.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.5 .sectionLabel}** If feasible, every
employer shall, by engineering controls or other physical means other
than hearing protectors, reduce an employee's exposure to a sound level
that does not exceed the limits referred to in section 7.4.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/94-33, s. 2(F)
- SOR/98-589, s. 4
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 27](#1177375-1188012){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177375-1188012.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 7.5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Report to Head of Compliance and Enforcement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893813 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2002-208, s. 11
- SOR/2014-148, s. 4
- SOR/2021-118, s. 6

\]
:::

**[[7.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.6 .sectionLabel}** If it is not feasible
for an employer, without providing hearing protectors to maintain an
employee's exposure to a sound level that does not exceed the limits
referred to in section 7.4, the employer shall, without delay,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} make a report in writing to the Head of Compliance
  and Enforcement setting out the reasons why it is not feasible to do
  so; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide a copy of the report to the work place
  committee or the health and safety representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/98-589, s. 5
- SOR/2002-208, s. 12
- SOR/2014-148, s. 5
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 28](#1177377-1188014){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177377-1188014.html"}
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312223){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312223.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.6-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 7.6"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Hearing Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893821 .Subheading}

- **[[7.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an employer is required to make a report in accordance with section
  7.6, the employer shall, as soon as feasible, provide every employee
  who is exposed to the sound level referred to in section 7.4 with a
  hearing protector that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements set out in CSA Standard
    Z94.2-M1984, Hearing Protectors, as amended from time to time; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevents the exposure to the sound level referred
    to in section 7.4.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer provides a hearing protector to an
  employee pursuant to subsection (1), the employer shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in consultation with the work place committee or
    the health and safety representative, formulate a program to train
    the employee in the fit, care and use of the hearing protector; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} implement the program.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that every person, other
  than an employee, to whom the employer grants access to a work place
  if the person is likely to be exposed to a sound level that exceeds
  the limits referred to in section 7.4 uses a hearing protector that
  meets the standard referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/94-33, s. 3
- SOR/98-589, s. 6
- SOR/2002-208, s. 13
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 29](#1177382-1188021){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177382-1188021.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 7.7"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Warning Signs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893832 .Subheading}

- **[[7.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  every work place where an employee may be exposed to an A-weighted
  sound pressure level greater than 87 dBA, the employer shall, at
  conspicuous locations within the work place, post and keep posted
  signs warning of a potentially hazardous level of sound in the work
  place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the measurement
  of the A-weighted sound pressure level in a work place shall be
  performed instantaneously, during normal working conditions, using the
  slow response setting of a sound level meter.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/91-448, s. 1
- SOR/98-589, s. 7
:::

## [PART VIII]{.HLabel1}[Electrical Safety]{.HTitleText1} {#h-893851 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893852 .Subheading}

**[[8.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[Canadian Electrical Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Canadian Electrical Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard C22.1-1990, Canadian Electrical
      Code, *Part I*, dated January 1990, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard C22.3 No.1-M1979, Overhead Systems
      and Underground Systems, dated April, 1979; ([Code canadien de
      l'électricité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[control device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[control device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device that will
    safely disconnect electrical equipment from its source of energy;
    ([dispositif de commande]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[electrical equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[electrical equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means equipment for the
    generation, distribution or use of electricity; ([outillage
    électrique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[guarantor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[guarantor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who gives a
    guarantee of isolation; ([garant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[guarded]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[guarded]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means covered, shielded, fenced,
    enclosed or otherwise protected by means of suitable covers or
    casings, barriers, guardrails, screens, mats or platforms to remove
    the possibility of dangerous contact or approach by persons or
    objects; ([protégé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[isolated]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[isolated]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means separated or disconnected
    from every source of electrical, hydraulic, pneumatic or other kind
    of energy that is capable of making electrical equipment dangerous;
    ([isolé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[person in charge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[person in charge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employee who
    supervises employees performing work on or a live test of isolated
    electrical equipment. ([responsable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 17(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 14
- SOR/98-427, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 30(F)](#1177389-1188025){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177389-1188025.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344391){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344391.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.1-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 8.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893865 .Subheading}

**[[8.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to the underground workings of mines.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 15
:::

### [Standards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893869 .Subheading}

- **[[8.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  design, construction and installation of all electrical equipment, if
  feasible, shall meet the standards set out in the Canadian Electrical
  Code, Part I.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operation and maintenance of all electrical
  equipment shall meet the standards set out in the Canadian Electrical
  Code.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 16
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 31](#1177391-1188026){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177391-1188026.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.3"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Safety Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893875 .Subheading}

- **[[8.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  testing or work performed on electrical equipment shall be performed
  by a qualified person or an employee under the direct supervision of a
  qualified person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the electrical equipment has a voltage in
  excess of 5,200 V between any two conductors or in excess of 3,000 V
  between any conductor and ground,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the qualified person or the employee referred to in
    subsection (1) shall use such insulated protection equipment and
    tools as will protect him from injury during the performance of the
    work; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee referred to in subsection (1) shall be
    instructed and trained in the use of the insulated protection
    equipment and tools.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 32(F)](#1177393-1188029){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177393-1188029.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.4"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[8.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  no employee shall work on electrical equipment unless the equipment is
  isolated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to isolate the electrical
  equipment and an employee is required to work on live equipment, the
  employer shall instruct the employee in procedures that are safe for
  live conductors.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where electrical equipment is not live but is capable
  of becoming live, no employee shall work on the equipment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} procedures that are safe for work on live equipment
    are used; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a safety ground is connected to the equipment.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (5) and (6), where an employee
  is working on or near electrical equipment that is live or may become
  live, the electrical equipment shall be guarded.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), if it is not feasible for
  the electrical equipment referred to in subsection (4) to be guarded,
  the employer shall take measures to protect the employee from injury
  by insulating the equipment from the employee or the employee from the
  ground.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where live electrical equipment is not guarded or
  insulated in accordance with subsection (4) or (5) or where the
  employee referred to in subsection (5) is not insulated from ground,
  the employee shall not work so near to any live part of the electrical
  equipment that is within a voltage range listed in column I of an item
  of the schedule to this Part that the distance between the body of the
  employee or any thing with which the employee is in contact and the
  live part of the equipment is less than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the distance set out in column II of that item,
    where the employee is not a qualified person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the distance set out in column III of that item,
    where the employee is a qualified person.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall work near a live part of any
  electrical equipment referred to in subsection (6) if there is a risk
  that an unintentional movement by the employee could bring any part of
  the body of the employee or any thing with which the employee is in
  contact closer to that live part than the distance referred to in that
  subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 18(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 17(F)
- SOR/98-427, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 14
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 33](#1177395-1188030){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177395-1188030.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.5"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[8.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.6 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall work
on or near high voltage electrical equipment unless he is authorized to
do so by his employer.

**[[8.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.7 .sectionLabel}** A legible sign with the
words "Danger --- High Voltage" and "Danger --- Haute Tension" in
letters that are not less than 50 mm in height on a contrasting
background shall be posted in a conspicuous place at every approach to
live high voltage electrical equipment.

### [Safety Watcher]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893897 .Subheading}

- **[[8.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee is working on or
  near live electrical equipment and, because of the nature of the work
  or the condition or location of the work place, it is necessary for
  the safety of the employee that the work be observed by a person not
  engaged in the work, the employer shall appoint a safety watcher

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to warn all employees in the work place of the
    hazard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that all safety precautions and
    procedures are complied with.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A safety watcher shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} informed of their duties as a safety watcher and of
    the hazard involved in the work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} trained and instructed in the procedures to follow
    in the event of an emergency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorized to stop immediately any part of the work
    that they consider dangerous; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} free of any other duties that might interfere with
    their duties as a safety watcher.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), an employer may
  appoint himself as a safety watcher.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 34](#1177406-1188035){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177406-1188035.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.8"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Coordination of Work]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893908 .Subheading}

**[[8.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.9 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee is
working on or in connection with electrical equipment, that employee and
every other person who is so working, including every safety watcher,
shall be fully informed by the employer with respect to the safe
coordination of their work.

### [Poles and Elevated Structures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893910 .Subheading}

- **[[8.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before an employee climbs a pole or
  elevated structure that is used to support electrical equipment, the
  employer shall give instructions and training to the employee
  respecting inspections and tests of the pole or structure to be
  carried out before the pole or structure is climbed.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, as a result of an inspection or test of a pole
  or elevated structure referred to in subsection (1), it appears to an
  employee that the pole or structure will be safe for climbing only
  when temporary supports have been installed, pike-poles alone shall
  not be used for such supports.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall work on any pole or elevated
  structure referred to in subsection (1) unless they have been
  instructed and trained in the rescue of employees who may be injured
  in the course of the work.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 35](#1177408-1188042){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177408-1188042.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.10-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.10"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[8.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.11 .sectionLabel}** Every pole or
elevated structure that is embedded in the ground and is used to support
electrical equipment shall meet the standards set out in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN3-015-M83, Wood Utility Poles and
  Reinforcing Studs, dated January, 1983; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard A14-M1979, Concrete Poles, the English
  version of which is dated September 1979 and the French version of
  which is dated November 1987.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 18
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344392){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344392.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.11"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Isolation of Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893920 .Subheading}

- **[[8.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before an employee isolates
  electrical equipment or changes or terminates the isolation of
  electrical equipment, the employer shall issue written instructions
  with respect to the procedures to be followed for the safe performance
  of that work.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall
  be signed by the employer and shall specify

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the instructions are issued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour of the commencement and of the
    termination of the period during which the instructions are to be
    followed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of the employee to whom the instructions
    are issued; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the instructions are in respect of the
    operation of a control device that affects the isolation of the
    electrical equipment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the device to which the instructions apply, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where applicable, the correct sequence of
      procedures.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the instructions referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be shown and explained to the employee.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall
  be kept readily available for examination by employees for the period
  referred to in paragraph (2)(b) and thereafter shall be kept by the
  employer for a period of one year at his place of business nearest to
  the work place in which the electrical equipment is located.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 19(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 36(F)](#1177413-1188044){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177413-1188044.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.12"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[8.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), no work
  on or live test of isolated electrical equipment shall be performed
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} isolation of the equipment has been confirmed by
    test; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer has determined, on the basis of visual
    observation, that every control device and every locking device
    necessary to establish and maintain the isolation of the equipment

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is set in the safe position with the
      disconnecting contacts of control devices safely separated or, in
      the case of a draw-out type electrical switch gear, is withdrawn
      to its full extent from the contacts of the electrical switch
      gear,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is locked out, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} bears a distinctive tag or sign designed to
      notify persons that operation of the control device and movement
      of the locking device are prohibited during the performance of the
      work or live test.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where more than one employee is performing any work
  on or live test of isolated electrical equipment, a separate tag or
  sign for each such employee shall be attached to each control device
  and locking device referred to in subsection (1).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The tag or sign referred to in subparagraph
  (1)(b)(iii) or subsection (2) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contain the words "DO NOT OPERATE --- [DÉFENSE
    D'ACTIONNER]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or display a symbol conveying
    the same meaning;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} show the date and hour that the control device and
    the locking device referred to in paragraph (1)(b) were set in the
    safe position or were withdrawn to their full extent from the
    contacts;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} show the name of the employee performing the work
    or live test;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where used in connection with a live test, be
    distinctively marked as a testing tag or sign;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} be removed only by the employee performing the work
    or live test; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} be used for no purpose other than the purpose
    referred to in paragraph (1)(b)(iii).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, because of the nature of the work in which the
  electrical equipment is being used, it is not feasible to comply with
  subsection (1), no work on or live test of electrical equipment shall
  be performed unless a guarantee of isolation referred to in section
  8.14 is given to the person in charge.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 20(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 19(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 37](#1177415-1188045){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177415-1188045.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.13-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.13"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Guarantees of Isolation for Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893952 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/88-632, s. 21(F)

\]
:::

- **[[8.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  employee shall give or receive a guarantee of isolation for electrical
  equipment unless he is authorized in writing by his employer to give
  or receive a guarantee of isolation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Not more than one employee shall give a guarantee of
  isolation for a piece of electrical equipment for the same period of
  time.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before an employee performs work on or a live test of
  isolated electrical equipment, the person in charge shall receive from
  the guarantor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a written guarantee of isolation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if owing to an emergency it is not feasible for the
    person in charge to receive a written guarantee of isolation, a
    non-written guarantee of isolation.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A written guarantee of isolation referred to in
  paragraph (3)(a) shall be signed by the guarantor and by the person in
  charge and shall contain the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the guarantee of isolation
    is given to the person in charge;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the electrical equipment
    will become isolated;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the isolation will be
    terminated, if known;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the procedures by which isolation is assured;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the guarantor and the person in charge;
    and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a statement as to whether live tests are to be
    performed.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a non-written guarantee of isolation referred
  to in paragraph (3)(b) is given, a written record thereof shall
  forthwith

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be made by the guarantor; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be made and signed by the person in charge.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A written record referred to in subsection (5) shall
  contain the information referred to in subsection (4).

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every written guarantee of isolation and every
  written record referred to in subsection (5) shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} kept by the person in charge readily available for
    examination by the employee performing the work or live test until
    the work or live test is completed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} given to the employer when the work or live test is
    completed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} kept by the employer for a period of one year after
    the completion of the work or live test at his place of business
    nearest to the work place in which the electrical equipment is
    located.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, ss. 21, 22
- SOR/94-263, s. 20(F)
- SOR/96-525, s. 13
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 38](#1177417-1188047){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177417-1188047.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.14-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.14"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[8.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.15 .sectionLabel}** Where a written
guarantee of isolation or a written record of an oral guarantee of
isolation is given to a person in charge and the person in charge is
replaced at the work place by another person in charge before the
guarantee has terminated, the other person in charge shall sign the
written guarantee of isolation or written record of the oral guarantee
of isolation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 23(F)
:::

**[[8.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.16 .sectionLabel}** Before an employee
gives a guarantee of isolation for electrical equipment that obtains all
or any portion of its electrical energy from a source that is not under
his direct control, the employee shall obtain a guarantee of isolation
in respect of the source from the person who is in direct control
thereof and is authorized to give the guarantee in respect thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 23(F)
:::

### [Live Test]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893984 .Subheading}

- **[[8.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  employee shall give a guarantee of isolation for the performance of a
  live test on isolated electrical equipment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any other guarantee of isolation given in respect
    of the electrical equipment for any part of the period for which the
    guarantee of isolation is given is terminated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every person to whom the other guarantee of
    isolation referred to in paragraph (a) was given has been informed
    of its termination; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any live test to be performed on the electrical
    equipment will not be hazardous to the health or safety of the
    person performing the live test.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person performing a live test shall warn all
  persons who, during or as a result of the test, are likely to be
  exposed to a hazard.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 23(F)
- SOR/2002-208, s. 39
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 39(F)](#1177419-1188049){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177419-1188049.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.17"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Termination of Guarantee of Isolation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-893993 .Subheading}

- **[[8.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The guarantee of isolation ends when
  work on, or a live test of, isolated electrical equipment is completed
  and the following conditions are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every person in charge,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has informed the guarantor that the work or live
      test is completed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has made and signed a record containing the date
      and hour when they informed the guarantor and the name of the
      guarantor; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on receipt of the information referred to in
    paragraph (a), the guarantor made and signed a record containing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour when the work or live test was
      completed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person in charge.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The records referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(ii)
  and paragraph (1)(b) shall be kept by the employer at the employer's
  place of business nearest to the work place in which the electrical
  equipment was located when it was isolated, for a period of one year
  after the day on which they are signed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 24(F)
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 4](#1299269-1300245){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299269-1300245.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.18-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.18"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Safety Grounding]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894004 .Subheading}

- **[[8.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  employee shall attach a safety ground to electrical equipment unless
  he has tested the electrical equipment and has established that it is
  isolated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of
  electrical equipment that is grounded by means of a grounding switch
  that is an integral part of the equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 25(F)
:::

- **[[8.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.20
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Work must not be performed on any
  electrical equipment in an area if the following devices are found
  unless the device is connected to a common grounding network:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a grounding bus,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a station grounding network,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a neutral conductor,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} temporary phase grounding, or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a metal structure.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, after the connections are made, a safety ground
  is required to ensure the safety of an employee working on the
  electrical equipment, the safety ground shall be connected to the
  common grounding network.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 40](#1177421-1188051){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177421-1188051.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.20-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.20"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[8.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.21 .sectionLabel}** Every conducting part
of a safety ground on isolated electrical equipment shall have
sufficient current carrying capacity to conduct the maximum current that
is likely to be carried on any part of the equipment for such time as is
necessary to permit operation of any device that is installed on the
electrical equipment so that, in the event of a short circuit or other
electrical current overload, the electrical equipment is automatically
disconnected from its source of electrical energy.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 26(F)
:::

- **[[8.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2),
  a

  [[point of safety grounding]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[point of safety grounding]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a grounding bus, a station grounding network, a
        neutral conductor, a metal structure or an aerial ground, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one or more metal rods that are not less than
        16 mm in diameter and are driven not less than l m into
        undisturbed compact earth at a minimum distance of 4.5 m from
        the base of the pole, structure, apparatus or other thing to
        which the electrical equipment is attached or from the area
        where persons on the ground work and in a direction away from
        the main work area. ([point de mise à la terre]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No safety ground shall be attached to or disconnected
  from isolated electrical equipment except in accordance with the
  following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground shall, if feasible, be attached
    to the pole, structure, apparatus or other thing to which the
    electrical equipment is attached;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all isolated conductors, neutral conductors and all
    non-insulated surfaces of the electrical equipment shall be
    short-circuited, electrically bonded together and attached by a
    safety ground to a point of safety grounding in a manner that
    establishes equal voltage on all surfaces that can be touched by
    persons who work on the electrical equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground shall be attached by means of
    mechanical clamps that are tightened securely and are in direct
    contact with bare metal;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground shall be so secured that none of
    its parts can make contact accidentally with any live electrical
    equipment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground shall be attached and
    disconnected using insulated protection equipment and tools;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground shall, before it is attached to
    isolated electrical equipment, be attached to a point of safety
    grounding; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the safety ground shall, before being disconnected
    from the point of safety grounding, be removed from the isolated
    electrical equipment in such a manner that the employee avoids
    contact with all live conductors.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 27
- SOR/94-263, s. 21(F)
- SOR/98-427, s. 3(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 41](#1177427-1188056){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177427-1188056.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.22-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.22"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Switches and Control Devices]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894037 .Subheading}

- **[[8.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every control device shall be so
  designed and located as to permit quick and safe operation at all
  times.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The path of access to every electrical switch,
  control device or meter shall be free from obstruction.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an electrical switch or other device
  controlling the supply of electrical energy to electrical equipment is
  operated only by a person authorized to do so by the employer, the
  switch or other device shall be fitted with a locking device that only
  an authorized person can activate.
