## [PART IX]{.HLabel1}[Sanitation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-894052 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894053 .Subheading}

**[[9.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[ARI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ARI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Air-Conditioning and
    Refrigeration Institute of the United States;
    ([ARI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Canadian Plumbing Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Canadian Plumbing Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2024-118, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

[[field accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[field accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means fixed or mobile
    accommodation that is living, eating or sleeping quarters provided
    by an employer for the accommodation of employees at a work place;
    ([logement sur place]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[mobile accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[mobile accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means field
    accommodation that may be easily and quickly moved. ([logement
    mobile]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[PSAI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[PSAI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Portable Sanitation
    Association International. ([PSAI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 1](#1455595-1498719){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455595-1498719.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894059 .Subheading}

- **[[9.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall maintain each
  personal service room and food preparation area used by employees in a
  clean and sanitary condition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Personal service rooms and food preparation areas
  shall be so used by employees that the rooms or areas will remain as
  clean and in such a sanitary condition as is possible.

**[[9.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.3 .sectionLabel}** All janitorial work
that may cause dusty or unsanitary conditions shall be carried out in a
manner that will prevent the contamination of the air by dust or other
substances injurious to health.

**[[9.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.4 .sectionLabel}** Each personal service
room shall be cleaned at least once every day that it is used.

- **[[9.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every plumbing system that supplies
  potable water and removes water-borne waste

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall meet the standards set out in the National
    Plumbing Code of Canada 2020, developed by the Canadian Commission
    on Building and Fire Codes and published by the National Research
    Council of Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), shall be connected to a
    municipal sanitation sewer or water main.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to comply with paragraph
  (1)(b), the employer shall provide a waste disposal system that meets
  the standards set out in ANSI/PSAI standard Z4.3-2016, entitled
  Sanitation - Nonsewered Waste-Disposal Systems - Minimum Requirements,
  as amended from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 22(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 42](#1177432-1188058){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177432-1188058.html"}
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 2](#1455599-1498722){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455599-1498722.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.5-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 9.5"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[9.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  container that is used for solid or liquid waste in the work place
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be equipped with a tight-fitting cover;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be so constructed that it can easily be cleaned and
    maintained in a sanitary condition;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be leak-proof; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where there may be internal pressure in the
    container, be so designed that the pressure is relieved by
    controlled ventilation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each container referred to in subsection (1) shall be
  emptied at least once every day that it is used.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 43(F)](#1177434-1188061){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177434-1188061.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.6-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.6"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[9.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  enclosed part of a work place, each personal service room and each
  food preparation area shall be constructed, equipped and maintained in
  a manner that will prevent the entrance of vermin.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where vermin have entered any enclosed part of a work
  place, personal service room or food preparation area, the employer
  shall immediately take all steps necessary to eliminate the vermin and
  prevent the re-entry of the vermin.

**[[9.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.8 .sectionLabel}** No person shall use a
personal service room for the purpose of storing equipment unless a
closet fitted with a door is provided in that room for that purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 28(F)
:::

**[[9.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.9 .sectionLabel}** In each personal
service room and food preparation area, the temperature, measured one
metre above the floor in the centre of the room or area, shall, if
feasible, be maintained at a level of not less than 18°C and not more
than 29°C.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 23(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 44](#1177436-1188062){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177436-1188062.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.9"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[9.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  each personal service room and food preparation area, the floors,
  partitions and walls shall be so constructed that they can be easily
  washed and maintained in a sanitary condition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The floor and lower 150 mm of any walls and
  partitions in any food preparation area or toilet room shall be
  water-tight and impervious to moisture.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 24(E)
:::

**[[9.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.11 .sectionLabel}** Where separate
personal service rooms are provided for employees of each sex, each room
shall be equipped with a door that is self-closing and is clearly marked
to indicate the sex of the employees for whom the room is provided.

### [Toilet Rooms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894094 .Subheading}

- **[[9.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  feasible, a toilet room shall be provided for employees and, subject
  to sections 9.13 and 9.131, if persons of both sexes are employed at
  the same work place, a separate toilet room shall be provided for
  employees of each sex.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), where a toilet
  room is provided in accordance with subsection (1), the employer shall
  provide in that room a number of toilets determined according to the
  maximum number of employees of each sex who are normally employed at
  any one time at the work place as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the number of such employees does not exceed
    nine, one toilet;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the number of such employees exceeds nine but
    does not exceed 24, two toilets;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the number of such employees exceeds 24 but
    does not exceed 49, three toilets;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the number of such employees exceeds 49 but
    does not exceed 74, four toilets;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} where the number of such employees exceeds 74 but
    does not exceed 100, five toilets; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} where the number of such employees exceeds 100,
    five toilets and one toilet for every 30 such employees or portion
    of that number in excess of 100.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), where the class of
  employment in a work place is the transaction of business or the
  rendering of professional or personal services, the number of toilets
  provided by the employer in accordance with subsection (2) may be
  reduced

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the number of employees of each sex does not
    exceed 25, to one toilet;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the number of employees of each sex exceeds
    25 but does not exceed 50, to two toilets; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the number of employees of each sex exceeds
    50, to three toilets and one toilet for every 50 employees or
    portion of that number in excess of 50.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employer may substitute urinals for up to
  two-thirds of the number of toilets required by subsection (2) or (3)
  to be provided for male employees.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employee shall not be counted for the purposes of
  subsections (2) and (3) if they are normally away from the work place
  for more than 75% of their working time and they do not normally use
  the toilet room in the work place.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} If feasible, toilet rooms and wash basins separate
  from those used by other employees shall be provided for food
  handlers.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 29(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 45(E)](#1177438-1188064){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177438-1188064.html"}
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 3](#1455604-1498726){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455604-1498726.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.12-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 9.12"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[9.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  employer may provide only one toilet for all employees if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total number of employees normally employed at
    the work place at any one time does not exceed five; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door of the toilet room is fitted on the inside
    with a locking device.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the class of employment in the work place is the
  transaction of business or the rendering of professional or personal
  services, the employer may provide only one toilet for all employees
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total number of employees normally employed at
    the work place at any one time does not exceed 10 or the area of the
    work place does not exceed 100 m^2^; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the door of the toilet room is fitted on the inside
    with a locking device.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 30(F)
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 4](#1455611-1498731){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455611-1498731.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.13-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.13"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[9.131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.131 .sectionLabel}** A toilet that is
required to be provided by the employer under section 9.12 may be
provided in an all-gender toilet room.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 5](#1455620-1498734){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455620-1498734.html"}
:::

**[[9.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.14 .sectionLabel}** Toilet rooms shall be
located not more than 60 m from and not more than one storey above or
below each work place.

**[[9.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.15 .sectionLabel}** Every toilet room
shall be so designed that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is completely enclosed with solid material that is
  non-transparent from the outside;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no toilet or urinal is visible when the door of the
  toilet room is open;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} it has a ceiling height of not less than 2.2 m;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the toilet room contains more than one toilet,
  each toilet is enclosed in a separate compartment fitted with a door
  and an inside locking device; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the walls of each separate toilet compartment are
  designed and constructed to provide a reasonable amount of privacy for
  its occupant.

**[[9.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.16 .sectionLabel}** Toilet paper on a
holder or in a dispenser shall be provided

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where there is only one toilet in a toilet room, in
  that toilet room; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in each toilet compartment.

<!-- -->

- **[[9.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall provide menstrual
  products, including clean and hygienic tampons and menstrual pads, in
  each toilet room.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to provide menstrual products
  in a toilet room, the employer shall provide them in another location
  in the same work place that is controlled by the employer, accessible
  by employees at all times and offers a reasonable amount of privacy.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall provide a covered container for
  the disposal of menstrual products

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in any toilet room that has only one toilet; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in each toilet compartment of any toilet room that
    has more than one toilet.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-78, s. 1](#1397684-1430594){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1397684-1430594.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.17"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Wash Basins]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894133 .Subheading}

**[[9.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.18 .sectionLabel}** Hot water provided
for personal washing

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be maintained at a temperature of not less than
  35°C and not more than 43°C; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall not be heated by mixing with steam.

<!-- -->

- **[[9.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 9.20 and 9.21,
  every employer shall provide for each toilet room wash basins supplied
  with cold water and hot water that meets the requirements of section
  9.18 as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the room contains one or two toilets or
    urinals, one wash basin; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the room contains more than two toilets or
    urinals, one wash basin for every two toilets or urinals.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an outdoor privy is provided by an employer, the
  employer shall provide wash basins required by subsection (1) as close
  as possible to the outdoor privy.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 31
- SOR/94-263, s. 25(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 46](#1177443-1188067){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177443-1188067.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.19-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.19"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[9.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.20 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section
9.21, where a toilet room is provided and the work environment of
employees is such that their health is likely to be endangered by a
hazardous substance coming into contact with their skin, the employer
shall provide a wash room with individual wash basins supplied with cold
water and hot water that meets the requirements of section 9.18 as
follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the number of those employees does not exceed
  five, one wash basin;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the number of those employees exceeds five but
  does not exceed 10, two wash basins;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the number of those employees exceeds 10 but
  does not exceed 15, three wash basins;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the number of those employees exceeds 15 but
  does not exceed 20, four wash basins; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} where the number of those employees exceeds 20, four
  wash basins and one additional wash basin for every 15 of those
  employees or portion of that number in excess of 20.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 32(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 65(F)
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 47(F)](#1177445-1188069){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177445-1188069.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.20-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.20"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[9.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.21 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  industrial wash trough or circular wash basin of a capacity equivalent
  to the aggregate of the minimum standard capacities of the wash basins
  referred to in sections 9.19 and 9.20 may be provided in place of the
  wash basins.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An industrial wash trough or circular wash basin
  referred to in subsection (1) shall be supplied with cold water and
  hot water that meets the requirements of section 9.18.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 26
:::

**[[9.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.22 .sectionLabel}** In every personal
service room that contains a wash basin, the employer shall provide

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} powdered or liquid soap or other cleaning agent in a
  dispenser at each wash basin or between adjoining wash basins;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} sufficient sanitary hand drying facilities to serve
  the number of employees using the personal service room; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a non-combustible container for the disposal of used
  towels where towels are provided.

### [Showers and Shower Rooms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894161 .Subheading}

- **[[9.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  shower room with a door fitted on the inside with a locking device and
  at least one shower head for every 10 employees or portion of that
  number shall be provided for employees who regularly perform strenuous
  physical work in a high temperature or high humidity or whose bodies
  may be contaminated by a hazardous substance.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every shower receptor shall be constructed and
  arranged in such a way that water cannot leak through the walls or
  floors.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No more than six shower heads shall be served by a
  single shower drain.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where two or more shower heads are served by a shower
  drain, the floor shall be sloped and the drain so located that water
  from one head cannot flow over the area that serves another head.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Except for column showers, where a battery of shower
  heads is installed, the horizontal distance between two adjacent
  shower heads shall be at least 750 mm.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Waterproof finish shall be provided to a height of
  not less than 1.8 m above the floor in shower rooms and shall consist
  of ceramic, plastic or metal tile, sheet vinyl, tempered hardboard,
  laminated thermosetting decorative sheets or linoleum.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Finished flooring in shower rooms shall consist of
  resilient flooring, felted-synthetic fibre floor coverings, concrete
  terrazzo, ceramic tile, mastic or other types of flooring providing
  similar degrees of water resistance.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} Where duck boards are used in showers, they shall not
  be made of wood.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} Every shower shall be provided with cold water and
  hot water that meets the requirements of section 9.18.

- [(10)]{.lawlabel} An employee referred to in subsection (1) who takes
  a shower as a result of their work shall be provided with a clean
  towel and soap or another cleaning agent.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 33(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 65(F)
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 48(F)](#1177447-1188070){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177447-1188070.html"}
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 6(E)](#1455622-1498736){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455622-1498736.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.23-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 9.23"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Potable Water]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894175 .Subheading}

**[[9.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.24 .sectionLabel}** Every employer shall
provide potable water for drinking, personal washing and food
preparation that conforms to the Guidelines for Canadian Drinking Water
Quality, published by the Department of Health, as amended from time to
time.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 7](#1455624-1498739){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455624-1498739.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.24-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.24"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[9.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.25 .sectionLabel}** Where it is necessary
to transport water for drinking, personal washing or food preparation,
only sanitary portable water containers shall be used.

**[[9.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.26 .sectionLabel}** Where a portable
storage container for drinking water is used,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the container shall be securely covered and closed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the container shall be used only for the purpose of
  storing potable water;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the container shall not be stored in a toilet room;
  and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the water shall be drawn from the container by

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a tap,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a ladle used only for the purpose of drawing water
    from the container, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any other means that precludes the contamination
    of the water.

**[[9.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.27 .sectionLabel}** Except where drinking
water is supplied by a drinking fountain, sanitary single-use drinking
cups shall be provided.

**[[9.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.28 .sectionLabel}** Any ice that is added
to drinking water or used for the contact refrigeration of foodstuffs
shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be made from potable water; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} be so stored and handled as to prevent contamination.

**[[9.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.29 .sectionLabel}** A drinking fountain
that supplies drinking water shall meet the standards set out in the ARI
standard ARI 1010-2002, entitled Self-Contained, Mechanically-
Refrigerated Drinking-Water Coolers, as amended from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 8](#1455626-1498742){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455626-1498742.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.29-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.29"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Field Accommodation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894191 .Subheading}

**[[9.30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.30 .sectionLabel}** All field
accommodation shall meet the following standards:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} it shall be located on well-drained ground;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} it shall be so constructed that it can easily be
  cleaned and disinfected;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the food preparation area and lunch room shall be
  separated from the sleeping quarters;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} where a water plumbing system is provided, the system
  shall operate under sanitary conditions;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} garbage disposal facilities shall be provided to
  prevent the accumulation of garbage;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} toilet rooms shall be maintained in a sanitary
  condition; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} vermin prevention, heating, ventilation and sanitary
  sewage systems shall be provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 27(F)
:::

- **[[9.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Living quarters provided

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in any fixed accommodation shall comprise

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a single occupant, a space of at least 18
      m^3^, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where there is more than one occupant, 18 m^3^
      plus 12 m^3^ for each additional occupant; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any mobile accommodation shall comprise

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a single occupant, a space of at least 12
      m^3^, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where there is more than one occupant, 12 m^3^
      plus 8 m^3^ for each additional occupant.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The living quarters referred to in subsection (1)
  shall have no floor dimension that is less than 1.5 m.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Toilet rooms and locker rooms shall not be counted in
  the calculation made in accordance with subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 34(F)
:::

**[[9.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.32 .sectionLabel}** Every mobile
accommodation shall meet the standards set out in chapter Z240.2.1-16,
entitled Technical requirements for manufactured homes, of the CSA
standard Z240 MH Series-16, entitled Manufactured Homes, as amended from
time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344394){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344394.html"}
- [SOR/2024-118, s. 9](#1455628-1498744){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1455628-1498744.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.32-20220502.html "Link to previous version of section 9.32"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[9.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.33 .sectionLabel}** In any field
accommodation provided as sleeping quarters for employees

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a separate bed or bunk shall be provided for each
  employee;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the beds or bunks shall not be more than
  double-tiered and shall be so constructed that they can be cleaned and
  disinfected;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} mattresses, sheets, pillow cases, blankets and bed
  covers shall be provided for each employee and kept in a clean and
  sanitary condition;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} clean laundered sheets and pillow cases shall be
  provided for each employee at least once each week; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} at least one shelf and a locker fitted with a locking
  device shall be provided for each employee.

### [Preparation, Handling, Storage and Serving of Food]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894223 .Subheading}

- **[[9.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each food handler shall be
  instructed and trained in food handling practices that prevent the
  contamination of food.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person who is suffering from a communicable
  disease shall work as a food handler.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 49(F)](#1177449-1188072){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177449-1188072.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.34-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.34"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[9.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.35 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2024-118, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.35-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.35"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[9.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where foods stored by an employer
  for consumption by employees require refrigeration to prevent them
  from becoming hazardous to health, the foods shall be maintained at a
  temperature of 4°C or lower.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where foods stored by an employer for consumption by
  employees require freezing to prevent them from becoming hazardous to
  health, the foods shall be maintained at a temperature of -11°C or
  lower.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 35
:::

**[[9.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.37 .sectionLabel}** All equipment and
utensils that come into contact with food shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} designed to be easily cleaned;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} smooth, free from cracks, crevices, pitting or
  unnecessary indentations; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} cleaned to maintain their surfaces in a sanitary
  condition.

**[[9.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.38 .sectionLabel}** No person shall eat,
prepare or store food

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a place where a hazardous substance may
  contaminate food, dishes or utensils;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a personal service room that contains a toilet,
  urinal or shower; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other place where food is likely to be
  contaminated.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 50(F)](#1177451-1188073){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177451-1188073.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.38-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.38"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Food Waste and Garbage]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894243 .Subheading}

- **[[9.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.39 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  food waste or garbage shall be stored in a food preparation area.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Food waste and garbage shall be handled and removed
  from a food preparation area or lunch room in accordance with
  subsections (3) to (5).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Wet food waste and garbage shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} disposed of by mechanical grinders or choppers
    connected to sewage disposal lines; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held in leak-proof, non-absorptive, easily-cleaned
    containers with tight-fitting covers in a separate enclosed area or
    container until removal for disposal.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Dry food waste and garbage shall be removed or
  incinerated.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Food waste and garbage containers shall be kept
  covered and the food waste and garbage removed as frequently as is
  necessary to prevent unsanitary conditions.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Food waste and garbage containers shall, each time
  they are emptied, be cleansed and disinfected in an area separate from
  the food preparation area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 36(F)
:::

### [Lunch Rooms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894255 .Subheading}

**[[9.40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.40 .sectionLabel}** Every lunch room
provided by the employer

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be separated from any place where a hazardous
  substance may contaminate food, dishes or utensils;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall not be used for any purpose that is
  incompatible with its use as a lunch room;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall not have any dimension of less than 2.3 m;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} shall have a minimum floor area of 9 m^2^;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} shall have 1.1 m^2^ of floor area for each of the
  employees who normally use the room at any one time;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} shall be furnished with a sufficient number of tables
  and seats to accommodate adequately the number of employees normally
  using the lunch room at any one time; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} shall be provided with non-combustible covered
  receptacles for the disposal of waste food or other waste material.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 37(F)
- SOR/2001-321, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
:::

### [Ventilation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894266 .Subheading}

- **[[9.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.41
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each personal service room and food
  preparation area shall be ventilated to provide at least two changes
  of air per hour

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by mechanical means, where the room is normally
    used by 10 or more employees at any one time; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by mechanical means or natural ventilation through
    a window or similar opening, where the room is used by fewer than 10
    employees if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the window or similar opening is located on an
      outside wall of the room, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} not less than 0.2 m^2^ of unobstructed
      ventilation is provided for each of the employees who normally use
      the room at any one time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer provides ventilation by mechanical
  means in accordance with paragraph (1)(a), the amount of air provided
  for a type of room set out in Column I of an item of the schedule to
  this Part shall be not less than that set out in Column II of that
  item.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer provides for the ventilation of a
  food preparation area or a lunch room by mechanical means in
  accordance with paragraph (1)(a), the rate of change of air shall be
  not less than nine litres per second for each employee who is normally
  employed in the food preparation area at any one time or for each
  employee who uses the lunch room at any one time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 38(F)
:::

- **[[9.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.42
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), any
  exhaust system from a personal service room containing a toilet or a
  shower shall not be connected with any other exhaust or air supply
  system.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The exhaust system for a personal service room
  containing a toilet or shower may be connected with the exhaust duct
  of another room at the exhaust fan inlet if the system is connected in
  such a manner that an exchange of air cannot occur between the rooms.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 39
:::

### [Clothing Storage]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894282 .Subheading}

**[[9.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.43 .sectionLabel}** Clothing storage
facilities shall be provided by the employer for the storage of
overcoats and outer clothes not worn by employees while they are
working.

- **[[9.44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  change room shall be provided by the employer where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the work engaged in by an employee
    makes it necessary for that employee to change from street clothes
    to work clothes for health or safety reasons; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an employee is regularly engaged in work in which
    his work clothing becomes wet or contaminated by a hazardous
    substance.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where wet or contaminated work clothing referred to
  in paragraph (1)(b) is changed, it shall be stored in such a manner
  that it does not come in contact with clothing that is not wet or
  contaminated.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall leave the work place wearing
  clothing contaminated by a hazardous substance.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall supply drying and cleaning
  facilities for the purpose of drying or cleaning wet or contaminated
  clothing referred to in paragraph (1)(b).

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} In each change room,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a floor area of at least 0.4 m^2^ shall be provided
    for each of the employees who normally use the room at any one time;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it is necessary for the employees to change
    footwear, seats shall be provided in sufficient numbers to
    accommodate them.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 40(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 28(F)
- SOR/2002-208, ss. 42, 43(F)
:::

**[[9.45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.45 .sectionLabel}** If feasible, the
clothing storage facilities referred to in section 9.43 and the change
room referred to in section 9.44 shall be located

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} near the work place and connected thereto by a
  completely covered route;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on a direct route to the entrance to the work place;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} near a shower room provided pursuant to section 9.23;
  and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} near a toilet room.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 51(E)](#1177453-1188075){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177453-1188075.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.45-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.45"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART X]{.HLabel1}[Hazardous Substances]{.HTitleText1} {#h-894311 .Part}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)

\]
:::

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894314 .Subheading}

**[[10.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[airborne asbestos fibres]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[airborne asbestos fibres]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means asbestos
    fibres that are longer than 5 μm (micrometres) with an aspect ratio
    equal to or greater than 3:1 and that are carried by the
    air; ([fibres d'amiante aéroportées]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[airborne chrysotile asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[airborne chrysotile asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2017-132, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

[[asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[asbestos]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means actinolite, amosite,
    anthophyllite, chrysotile, crocidolite and tremolite in their
    fibrous form; ([amiante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[asbestos-containing material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[asbestos-containing material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any article that is manufactured and contains 1%
      or more asbestos by weight at the time of manufacture or that
      contains a concentration of 1% or more asbestos as determined in
      accordance with Method 9002 set out in the document entitled NIOSH
      Manual of Analytical Methods, published by the National Institute
      for Occupational Safety and Health, as amended from time to time,
      or in accordance with a scientifically proven method used to
      collect and analyze a representative sample of the material; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any material that contains a concentration of 1%
      or more asbestos as determined in accordance with Method 9002 set
      out in the document entitled NIOSH Manual of Analytical Methods,
      published by the National Institute for Occupational Safety and
      Health, as amended from time to time, or in accordance with a
      scientifically proven method used to collect and analyze a
      representative sample of the material; ([matériau contenant de
      l'amiante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[clearance air sampling]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[clearance air sampling]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the action of
    taking samples to determine if the concentration of airborne
    asbestos fibres inside an enclosure is below the limit referred to
    in section 10.19 to permit the dismantling of a containment
    system; ([échantillonnage de l'air après
    décontamination]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[containment system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[containment system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an isolation system
    that is designed to effectively contain asbestos fibre within a
    designated work area where asbestos-containing material is handled,
    removed, encapsulated or enclosed; ([système de
    confinement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[encapsulation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[encapsulation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the treatment of an
    asbestos-containing material with a sealant that penetrates the
    material and binds the asbestos fibres together, and the treatment
    of the surface of the asbestos-containing material with a sealant
    that creates a membrane on the surface, to prevent the release of
    asbestos fibres into the air; ([encapsulation]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[enclosure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[enclosure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a physical barrier such as
    drywall, plywood or metal sheeting that, as part of the containment
    system, isolates asbestos-containing material from adjacent areas in
    a building to prevent the release of airborne asbestos fibres into
    those areas; ([encloisonnement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[friable]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[friable]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of
    asbestos-containing material, that the material, when dry, can be
    easily crumbled or powdered by hand
    pressure; ([friable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[glove bag]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[glove bag]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a polyethylene or polyvinyl
    chloride bag that is affixed around an asbestos-containing source
    and that permits asbestos-containing material to be removed while
    minimizing the release of airborne asbestos fibres into the work
    place; ([sac à gants]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hazard information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hazard information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
    hazardous substance, information on the proper and safe storage,
    handling, use and disposal of the hazardous substance, including
    information relating to the health and physical hazards that it
    presents; ([renseignements sur les risques]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[HEPA filter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[HEPA filter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a high-efficiency
    particulate air filter that has been tested to ensure efficiency
    equal to or exceeding 99.97% for removal of airborne particles
    having a mean aerodynamic diameter of 0.3 µm (micrometres) from the
    air; ([filtre HEPA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[high-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[high-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an activity that
    involves the handling or disturbance of friable asbestos-containing
    material or is carried out in proximity to friable
    asbestos-containing material, that requires a high level of control
    to prevent exposure to excessive concentrations of airborne asbestos
    fibres and that includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the removal or disturbance of more than 1 m^2^ of
      friable asbestos-containing material in a work place, even if the
      activity is divided into smaller jobs,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the spray application of a sealant to a friable
      asbestos-containing material,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the cleaning or removal of air-handling
      equipment, other than filters, in a building that has sprayed-on
      fireproofing or sprayed-on thermal insulation that is
      asbestos-containing material,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the repair, alteration or demolition of all or
      part of a kiln, metallurgical furnace or similar structure that
      contains asbestos-containing material,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the activity is carried out by means of power tools
      that are not attached to dust-collecting devices equipped with
      HEPA filters, and

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the repair, alteration or demolition of all or
      part of a building in which asbestos is or was used in the
      manufacture of products, unless the asbestos was cleaned up and
      removed; ([activité à risque élevé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[low-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[low-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an activity that
    involves the handling of asbestos-containing material or is carried
    out in proximity to non-friable asbestos-containing material and
    that includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the installation or removal of ceiling tiles that
      are made of non-friable asbestos-containing material and cover an
      area of less than 7.5 m^2^,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the installation or removal of other non-friable
      asbestos-containing material, if the material is not being broken,
      cut, drilled, abraded, ground, sanded or vibrated and dust is not
      being generated,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the material is wetted to control the spread of dust
      or fibres and the activity is carried out only by means of
      non-powered hand-held tools, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the removal of less than 1 m² of drywall in which
      joint cement containing asbestos has been used; ([activité à
      faible risque]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[moderate-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[moderate-risk activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an activity
    that involves the handling of asbestos-containing material or is
    carried out in proximity to friable asbestos-containing material,
    that is not otherwise classified as a low-risk activity or high-risk
    activity and that includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the removal of all or part of a false ceiling to
      gain access to a work area, if asbestos-containing material is
      likely to be found on the surface of the false ceiling,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the removal or disturbance of 1 m^2^ or less of
      friable asbestos-containing material during repair, alteration,
      maintenance or demolition work in a work place,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the enclosure of friable asbestos-containing
      material,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the application of tape, sealant or other
      covering to pipe or boiler insulation that is asbestos-containing
      material,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the removal of ceiling tiles that are
      asbestos-containing material, if the tiles cover an area of
      greater than 2 m^2^ and are removed without being broken, cut,
      drilled, abraded, ground, sanded or vibrated,

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the material is not wetted to control the spread of
      dust or fibres and the activity is carried out only by means of
      non-powered hand-held tools,

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the removal of 1 m^2^ or more of drywall in which
      joint cement that is asbestos-containing material has been used,

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the breaking, cutting, drilling, abrading,
      grinding, sanding or vibrating of non-friable asbestos-containing
      material, if the activity is carried out by means of power tools
      that are attached to dust-collecting devices equipped with HEPA
      filters,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the removal of insulation that is
      asbestos-containing material from a pipe, duct or similar
      structure using a glove bag, and

    - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the cleaning or removal of filters used in
      air-handling equipment in a building that has sprayed-on
      fireproofing that is asbestos-containing material; ([activité à
      risque modéré]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[product identifier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[product identifier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
    in subsection 1(1) of the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17); ([identificateur de
    produit]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2025-79, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

[[supplier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[supplier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section
    2 of the [Hazardous Products
    Act](/eng/acts/H-3); ([fournisseur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[work activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[work activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any low-risk activity,
    moderate-risk activity or high-risk activity or any activity that is
    ancillary to that activity, and the supervision of that activity and
    that ancillary activity. ([activité de travail]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894359 .Subheading}

**[[10.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to the handling or transportation of dangerous goods to which the
[Transportation of Dangerous Goods Act, 1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01) and
regulations made thereunder apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, ss. 5, 14
- SOR/94-263, s. 29
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
:::

### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-894363 .Subheading}

#### [Records of Hazardous Substances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894364 .Subheading}

**[[10.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.3 .sectionLabel}** Every employer shall
keep a record of all hazardous substances that are used, produced,
handled or stored for use in the work place and may either keep such a
record in the work place or keep a centralized record in respect of
several work places in one work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 30
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 53(E)](#1177457-1188078){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177457-1188078.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.3"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Hazard Investigation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894368 .Subheading}

- **[[10.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the health or safety of an employee is likely to be endangered by
  exposure to a hazardous substance in a work place, the employer shall,
  without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appoint a qualified person to carry out an
    investigation in that regard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of providing for the participation
    of the work place committee or the health and safety representative
    in the investigation, notify either of the proposed investigation
    and of the name of the qualified person appointed to carry out that
    investigation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In an investigation referred to in subsection (1),
  the following criteria shall be taken into consideration:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the chemical, biological and physical properties of
    the hazardous substance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the routes of exposure to the hazardous substance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the acute and chronic effects on health of exposure
    to the hazardous substance;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of the hazardous substance to be
    handled;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the hazardous substance is
    stored, used, handled and disposed of;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the control methods used to eliminate or reduce
    exposure of employees to the hazardous substance;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the concentration or level of the hazardous
    substance to which an employee is likely to be exposed;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} whether the concentration of an airborne chemical
    agent or the level of ionizing or non-ionizing radiation is likely
    to exceed 50 per cent of the values referred to in subsection
    10.19(1) or the levels referred to in subsections 10.26(3) and (4);
    and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether the level referred to in paragraph (g) is
    likely to exceed or be less than that prescribed in Part VI.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, ss. 15, 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 54](#1177459-1188080){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177459-1188080.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.4"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[10.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.5 .sectionLabel}** On completion of an
investigation referred to in subsection 10.4(1) and after consultation
with the work place committee or the health and safety representative,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the qualified person shall set out in a written
  report signed by the qualified person

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the qualified person's observations respecting the
    criteria considered in accordance with subsection 10.4(2), and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the qualified person's recommendations respecting
    the manner of compliance with sections 10.7 to 10.26, including
    recommendations respecting sampling and testing methods; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer shall establish and keep up-to-date
  written procedure for the controlling of the concentration or level of
  the hazardous substance in the work place and make it readily
  available for examination by employees in any form, as determined in
  consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
  committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/94-263, s. 31
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, ss. 16, 43(F)
- SOR/2016-141, s. 2
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 55](#1177464-1188082){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177464-1188082.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.5-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 10.5"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[10.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.6 .sectionLabel}** A report referred to
in section 10.5 shall be kept by the employer for a period of thirty
years after the date on which the qualified person signed the report.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
:::

#### [Medical Examinations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894395 .Subheading}

- **[[10.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a report referred to in section 10.5 recommends a medical examination
  for the employees likely to be exposed to a hazardous substance, the
  employer shall consult a physician to ascertain the necessity for that
  medical examination.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer, having consulted a physician pursuant
  to subsection (1) who has confirmed the necessity for a medical
  examination, shall not permit an employee to handle the hazardous
  substance in the work place unless a physician acceptable to the
  employee has examined the employee and declared the employee fit, or
  fit with specified restrictions, to handle the hazardous substance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the physician examining an employee pursuant to
  subsection (2) declares the employee fit with specified restrictions
  to handle the hazardous substance, the employer shall not permit the
  employee to handle the hazardous substance in the work place except in
  accordance with the specified restrictions.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer consults a physician pursuant to
  subsection (1), the employer shall keep a copy of the physician's
  decision with the report referred to in section 10.5.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The cost of a medical examination referred to in
  subsection (2) shall be borne by the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 56](#1177466-1188084){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177466-1188084.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.7"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Storage, Handling and Use]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894404 .Subheading}

**[[10.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.8 .sectionLabel}** Every hazardous
substance stored, handled or used in a work place shall be stored,
handled or used in a manner whereby the hazard related to that substance
is reduced to a minimum.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
:::

**[[10.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.9 .sectionLabel}** If a hazardous
substance is stored, handled or used in a work place, any hazard
resulting from that storage, handling or use shall be confined to as
small an area as possible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/94-263, s. 32(F)
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 57(E)](#1177468-1188086){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177468-1188086.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.9"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[10.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.10 .sectionLabel}** Every container for
a hazardous substance that is used in a work place shall be so designed
and constructed that it protects the employees from any health or safety
hazard that is caused by the hazardous substance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 42(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 33
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 17
:::

**[[10.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.11 .sectionLabel}** The quantity of a
hazardous substance for use or processing in a work place shall, if
feasible, be limited to the quantity required in one work day.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 58(E)](#1177470-1188088){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177470-1188088.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.11"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[10.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where, in a work place, a hazardous
  substance is capable of combining with another substance to form an
  ignitable combination and there exists a hazard of ignition of the
  combination by static electricity, the employer shall implement the
  standards set out in the United States National Fire Protection
  Association, Inc. publication NFPA 77, Recommended Practice on Static
  Electricity, dated 1988, as amended from time to time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of interpreting the standards
  referred to in subsection (1), [[acceptable]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means [[appropriate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 43
- SOR/94-263, s. 34
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
:::

#### [Warning of Hazardous Substances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894422 .Subheading}

**[[10.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.13 .sectionLabel}** Where a hazardous
substance is stored, handled or used in a work place, warnings shall be
given in appropriate places at access points warning every person
granted access to the work place of the presence of the hazardous
substance and of any precautions to be taken to prevent or reduce any
hazard of injury to health.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, ss. 6, 14
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/96-525, s. 14
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
:::

#### [Employee Education and Training]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894426 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2016-141, s. 3(E)

\]
:::

- **[[10.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall, in
  consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
  committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative, develop and implement an employee education and
  training program with respect to hazard prevention and control at the
  work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employee education and training program shall
  include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee who
    handles or is exposed to or who is likely to handle or be exposed to
    a hazardous substance, with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier of the hazardous
      substance,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} all hazard information disclosed by the supplier
      or by the employer on a safety data sheet or label,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all hazard information of which the employer is
      aware or ought to be aware,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the observations referred to in subparagraph
      10.5(a)(i),

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the information disclosed on a safety data sheet
      referred to in section 10.28 and the purpose and significance of
      that information, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} in respect of hazardous products in the work
      place, the information required to be disclosed on a safety data
      sheet and on a label under Division III and the purpose and
      significance of that information;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee who
    installs, operates, maintains or repairs an assembly of pipes or any
    other equipment referred to in section 10.24, with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} every valve and other control and safety device
      connected to the assembly of pipes,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow for the proper and safe
      use of the assembly of pipes, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the significance of the labelling,
      colour-coding, placarding or other modes of identification that
      are used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee who is
    referred to in paragraph (a) or (b), with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow to implement sections
      10.8, 10.9 and 10.12,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow for the safe storage,
      handling, use and disposal of hazardous substances, including
      procedures to be followed in an emergency involving a hazardous
      substance, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to follow if an employee is
      exposed to fugitive emissions as defined in section 10.29; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the education and training of each employee on the
    procedures to follow to access electronic or paper versions of
    reports, records of education and training given and safety data
    sheets.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall, in consultation with the policy
  committee or, if there is no policy committee, the work place
  committee or the health and safety representative, review and, if
  necessary, revise the employee education and training program

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least once a year;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whenever there is a change in conditions in respect
    of the presence of hazardous substances in the work place; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whenever new hazard information in respect of a
    hazardous substance in the work place becomes available to the
    employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, ss. 18, 43(F)
- SOR/2016-141, s. 4
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 59](#1177472-1188090){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177472-1188090.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.14-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 10.14"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[10.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.15 .sectionLabel}** The employer shall
keep a paper or electronic record of the education and training given to
every employee and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} make it readily available for examination by the
  employee in any form, as determined in consultation with the policy
  committee or, if there is no policy committee, the work place
  committee or the health and safety representative; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep it for a period of two years after the employee
  ceases

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to handle or be exposed to the hazardous substance,
    or is no longer likely to handle or be exposed to the hazardous
    substance, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to install, operate, maintain or repair the
    assembly of pipes.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- SOR/2016-141, s. 5
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 60](#1177474-1188092){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177474-1188092.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.15-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 10.15"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Substitution of Substances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894461 .Subheading}

**[[10.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.16 .sectionLabel}** No person shall use
a hazardous substance in a work place if a non-hazardous substance or
one that is less hazardous can be used instead.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, ss. 8, 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 44(F)
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 61](#1177479-1188094){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177479-1188094.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-10.16-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.16"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Ventilation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894467 .Subheading}

- **[[10.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every ventilation system installed
  on or after January 1, 1997 to control the concentration of an
  airborne hazardous substance shall be so designed, constructed,
  installed, operated and maintained that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of the airborne hazardous
    substance does not exceed the values and levels prescribed in
    subsections 10.19(1) and 10.20(1) and (2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it meets the standards set out in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Part 6 of the National Building Code,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the publication of the American Conference of
      Governmental Industrial Hygienists entitled Industrial
      Ventilation, 20th edition, dated 1988, as amended from time to
      time, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} ANSI Standard ANSI Z9.2-1979 entitled
      Fundamentals Governing the Design and Operation of Local Exhaust
      Systems, dated 1979, as amended from time to time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If feasible, every ventilation system installed
  before January 1, 1997 to control the concentration of an airborne
  hazardous substance shall be maintained so as to meet the requirements
  set out in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 9
- SOR/94-263, s. 35
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 62](#1177481-1188096){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177481-1188096.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.17"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[10.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before a ventilation system referred
  to in subsection 10.17(1) is operated for the first time in a work
  place, the employer shall set out in writing instructions pertaining
  to the inspection, testing and maintenance of that ventilation system.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall
  specify the nature and frequency of inspections, tests and maintenance
  to be performed on the ventilation system.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall ensure that a qualified person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} carries out each inspection, testing and
    maintenance of the ventilation system in accordance with the
    instructions referred to in subsection (1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} makes and signs a report with respect to each
    inspection, test or maintenance work.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A report referred to in paragraph (3)(b) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include the date of the inspection, test or
    maintenance work performed by the qualified person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the ventilation system that was inspected,
    tested or maintained; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the safety observations of the qualified
    person in respect of the ventilation system.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep at the work place at which
  the ventilation system is located a copy of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the instructions referred to in subsection (1), and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the most recent report referred to in paragraph
    (3)(b).

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall give to every employee who
  operates a ventilation system the necessary education and training for
  the safe and proper use of the system.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep a paper or electronic record
  of the education and training given to every employee who operates a
  ventilation system for as long as the employee remains in the
  employer's employ.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2016-141, s. 6
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 63(F)](#1177483-1188098){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177483-1188098.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-10.18-20160614.html "Link to previous version of section 10.18"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Control of Hazards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894495 .Subheading}

- **[[10.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee shall be kept free from
  exposure to a concentration of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an airborne chemical agent, other than airborne
    grain dust, airborne flour dust and airborne asbestos fibres, in
    excess of the value for that chemical agent adopted by the American
    Conference of Governmental Industrial Hygienists, in its publication
    entitled Threshold Limit Values (TLVs) and Biological Exposure
    Indices (BEIs), as amended from time to time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} airborne grain dust in excess of 4 mg/m^3^; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} airborne flour dust in excess of 3 mg/m^3^.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall ensure that an employee's
  exposure to a concentration of airborne asbestos fibres is as close to
  zero as possible, but in any event the employer shall ensure that the
  concentration does not exceed the value for airborne asbestos fibres
  adopted by the American Conference of Governmental Industrial
  Hygienists in its publication entitled Threshold Limit Values (TLV)
  and Biological Exposure Indices (BEI), as amended from time to time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of
  concentrations of carbon dioxide or respirable dust in the underground
  portion of a coal mine.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the concentration of an airborne chemical agent is
  likely to exceed the value referred to in subsection (1), air samples
  shall be taken and the concentration of the chemical agent shall be
  determined

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the standards set out by the
    American Conference of Governmental Industrial Hygienists in its
    publication entitled Manual of Analytical Methods Recommended for
    Sampling and Analysis of Atmospheric Contaminants, dated 1958, as
    amended from time to time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the standards set out by the
    United States National Institute for Occupational Safety and Health
    in the NIOSH Manual of Analytical Methods, third edition, volumes 1
    and 2, dated February, 1984, as amended from time to time;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with a method that collects and
    analyses a representative sample of the chemical agent with accuracy
    and with detection levels at least equal to those which would be
    obtained if the standards referred to in paragraph (a) or (b) were
    used; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where no specific standards for the chemical agent
    are set out in the publications referred to in paragraphs (a)
    and (b) and no method is available under paragraph (c), in
    accordance with a scientifically proven method used to collect and
    analyse a representative sample of the chemical agent.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A paper or electronic record of each test made under
  subsection (3) shall be kept by the employer at the employer's place
  of business that is nearest to the work place where the air sample was
  taken, for a period of three years after the date of the test.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A record referred to in subsection (4) shall include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the test;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazardous substance in respect of which the
    test was made;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the sampling and testing method used;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the result obtained; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name and occupation of the person who made the
    test.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, ss. 10, 14
- SOR/94-263, s. 37(F)

- **[[10.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.20
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  concentration of an airborne chemical agent or combination of airborne
  chemical agents in the work place shall be less than 50 per cent of
  the lower explosive limit of the chemical agent or combination of
  chemical agents.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a source of ignition may ignite an airborne
  chemical agent or combination of airborne chemical agents in the work
  place, the concentration of the chemical agent or the combination of
  chemical agents shall not exceed 10% of the lower explosive limit of
  the chemical agent or combination of chemical agents.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply in respect of
  concentrations of methane gas in the underground portion of a coal
  mine.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, ss. 11(E), 14(F)
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 3](#1520557-1524873){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520557-1524873.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.20-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.20"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[10.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.21 .sectionLabel}** Compressed air, gas
or steam shall not be used for blowing dust or other substances from
structures, machinery or materials where

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is a risk of any person being directly exposed
  to the jet or where a fire, explosion, injury or health hazard is
  likely to result from such use; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} that use would result in a concentration of an
  airborne chemical agent that exceeds the values referred to in
  paragraph 10.19(1)(a) or subsection 10.19(1.1) or the limit referred
  to in subsection 10.20(1) or (2).

- **[[10.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Compressed air shall not be used for
  cleaning clothing contaminated with:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} asbestos; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hazardous substance having an exposure limit
    referred to in paragraph 10.19(1)(a) or (b) lower than 1 mg/m^3^.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where compressed air is used to clean clothing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appropriate eye protection shall be worn; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum compressed air pressure in the pipeline
    shall be 69 kPa (10 psi) or a safety nozzle limiting the air
    pressure to no more than 69kPa (10 psi) shall be used.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 47
- SOR/90-180, s. 2
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
:::

#### [Warnings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894538 .Subheading}

**[[10.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.23 .sectionLabel}** If feasible, the
employer shall provide automated warning and detection systems if the
seriousness of any exposure to a hazardous substance so requires.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)

:::

#### [Assembly of Pipes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894542 .Subheading}

**[[10.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.24 .sectionLabel}** Every assembly of
pipes, pipe fittings, valves, safety devices, pumps, compressors and
other fixed equipment that is used for transferring a hazardous
substance from one location to another shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} marked, by labelling, colour-coding, placarding or
  any other mode, to identify the hazardous substance being transferred
  and, if appropriate, the direction of the flow; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fitted with valves and other control and safety
  devices to ensure its safe operation, maintenance and repair.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
- SOR/2016-141, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.24-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 10.24"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Explosives]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894548 .Subheading}

**[[10.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.25 .sectionLabel}** All blasting using
dynamite or other explosives shall be done by a qualified person who,
where required under the laws of the province in which the blasting is
carried out, holds a blasting certificate or such other authorization as
may be required under those laws.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-294, s. 2
:::

#### [Ionizing and Non-ionizing Radiation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-894552 .Subheading}

- **[[10.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a device that is capable of
  producing and emitting energy in the form of ionizing or non-ionizing
  radiation is used in the work place, the employer shall, if the device
  is referred to in subsection (2), implement the applicable document,
  as amended from time to time, published by the Department of National
  Health and Welfare, as specified in one of paragraphs (2)(a) to (k) or
  published by ANSI, as specified in paragraph (2)(l).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the applicable
  document is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of radio frequency and microwave devices
    in the frequency range from 10 kHz to 300 GHz, Safety Code - 6,
    dated 1990;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of X-ray equipment in medical diagnosis,
    Safety Code - 20A, dated 1980;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of baggage inspection X-ray equipment,
    Safety Code - 21, dated 1978;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in respect of dental X-ray equipment, Safety Code -
    22, dated 1980;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in respect of ultrasound equipment, Safety Code -
    23, dated 1989, and Safety Code - 24, dated 1990;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in respect of short-wave diathermy equipment,
    Safety Code - 25, dated 1983;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in respect of magnetic resonance imaging and
    magnetic resonance spectroscopy equipment, Safety Code - 26, dated
    1987;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in respect of industrial X-ray equipment, Safety
    Code - 27, dated 1987;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of veterinary X-ray equipment, Safety
    Code - 28, dated 1991;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} in respect of demonstration-type discharge devices,
    Recommended Safety Procedures for the Selection and Use of
    Demonstration-Type Discharge Devices in Schools, dated 1979;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} in respect of dielectric (RF) heaters, Dielectric
    (RF) Heaters Guidelines for Limiting Radio-Frequency Exposure, dated
    1980; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} in respect of lasers, ANSI Standard ANSI
    Z136.1-1986, American National Standard for the Safe Use of Lasers,
    dated 1986, including its appendices with the exception of Appendix
    D.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an employee works on or near a device that may
  emit nuclear energy, the employer shall ensure that the exposure of
  the employee to nuclear energy does not exceed the radiation dose
  limits set out in the [Radiation Protection
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2000-203).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} No employee, other than a nuclear energy worker as
  defined in section 2 of the [Nuclear Safety and Control
  Act](/eng/acts/N-28.3), shall be exposed in the course of any year to
  a concentration of radon that on average, over the year, is higher
  than 800 Bq/m^3^.
