[Previous
Version](section-11.12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.12"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Retention of Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1327144 .Subheading}

**[[11.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.13 .sectionLabel}** The employer must
keep at their place of business nearest to the work place in which the
confined space is located, and make readily available in paper or
electronic format,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any report made under subsection 11.03(2), the
  procedures established under subsection 11.04(1) and the emergency
  procedures established under paragraph 11.06(1)(a) for a period of ten
  years after the day on which the qualified person signed the report or
  the procedures were established;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any report made under subsection 11.05(3) for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a period of ten years after the day on which the
    qualified person signed the report if the verification procedures
    undertaken under paragraphs 11.05(1)(a) and (c) indicate that the
    requirements set out in subparagraphs 11.05(1)(a)(i) to (iii) were
    not complied with, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in every other case, for a period of two years
    after the day on which the qualified person signed the report; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any records of employees being trained under section
  11.12 for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} as long as the employee works for the employer; or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a period of two years after the day on which an
    employee ceases to perform any activity in a confined space.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-143, s. 1](#1303186-1327154){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1303186-1327154.html"}
:::

## [PART XII]{.HLabel1}[Protection Equipment and Other Preventive Measures]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895051 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1187507 .Subheading}

**[[12.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.01 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[CBRN agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[CBRN agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a chemical, biological,
    radiological or nuclear agent. ([agent CBRN]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[fall hazard zone]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fall hazard zone]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a defined area
    bordering on an edge that is unguarded against a risk of
    falling. ([zone de risque de chute]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fall hazard zone system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fall hazard zone system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a system that
    consists of a fall hazard zone that is supervised by a fall hazard
    zone monitor. ([système pour zone de risque de
    chute]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fall-arrest system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fall-arrest system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a collection of
    protection equipment that attaches a person to an anchorage and is
    designed and configured to arrest a free fall. ([dispositif
    antichute]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fall-protection system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fall-protection system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a system that
    is designed and configured to eliminate or reduce the risk of a
    person falling, restrain a person who is at risk of falling or
    arrest a person's fall. The system may be composed of one or more of
    the following:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a passive fall-protection system;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a fall-restraint system;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a fall-arrest system;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a fall hazard zone system. ([dispositif de
      protection contre les chutes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fall-restraint system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fall-restraint system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a collection of
    protection equipment that attaches a person to an anchorage and is
    designed and configured to prevent the person from getting close to
    an unguarded edge. ([dispositif de retenue contre les
    chutes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[passive fall-protection system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[passive fall-protection system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    system of physical barriers that is designed and installed to
    prevent a person from falling, including guardrails, fences,
    barricades or covers. ([dispositif passif de protection contre les
    chutes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[personal fall-protection system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[personal fall-protection system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    [[fall-restraint system]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or [[fall-arrest
    system]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}. ([dispositif individuel de
    protection contre les chutes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187540){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187540.html"}
:::

- **[[12.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise indicated in this
  Part, any reference in this Part to a standard is a reference to that
  standard as amended from time to time.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any amendment to a CSA Group standard, a CSA standard
  or a UL standard that is incorporated by reference in this Part is
  effective on the 30th day after the day on which the amendment is
  published by the CSA Group or by UL, as the case may be, in both
  official languages.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187548){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187548.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 2](#1343788-1344356){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343788-1344356.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.02-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 12.02"){.PITLink}
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1187552 .Subheading}

- **[[12.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.03
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to eliminate a
  health or safety hazard in a work place or to reduce it to within safe
  limits and the use of protection equipment may eliminate or reduce the
  risk of injury from that hazard, every person who is granted access to
  the work place and who is exposed to that hazard must use the
  protection equipment prescribed by this Part.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person who provides
  instruction and training in the provision of emergency rescue services
  or who is instructed and trained in the provision of those services
  and provides them in an emergency situation may use protection
  equipment other than the equipment that is prescribed by this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187565){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187565.html"}
:::

**[[12.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.04 .sectionLabel}** Any protection
equipment that is provided or used in a work place must be designed to
protect the person from the hazard in question and must not in itself
create a hazard.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187569){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187569.html"}
:::

- **[[12.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.05
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All protection equipment that is
  provided by an employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be properly stored and be maintained, inspected
    and, if applicable, tested by a qualified person in accordance with
    the manufacturer's instructions to ensure that it is in good
    operating condition;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of equipment that is worn by a person,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be safely and properly fitted to each user by a
      qualified person in accordance with the manufacturer's
      instructions, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if necessary to prevent a health hazard, be
      maintained in a clean and sanitary condition by a qualified person
      in accordance with the manufacturer's instructions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any component of the equipment that
    requires installation and dismantling, be installed and dismantled
    by a qualified person in accordance with the manufacturer's
    instructions; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of equipment that is used in a
    fall-protection system, be used in accordance with the
    manufacturer's instructions.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of equipment that is used in a
  fall-protection system, if there are no instructions from the
  manufacturer with respect to the storage, maintenance, inspection,
  testing, fitting, installation, use or dismantling referred to in
  paragraphs (1)(a) to (d), the equipment must be stored, maintained,
  inspected, tested, fitted, installed, used or dismantled in accordance
  with the fall-protection plan.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187580){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187580.html"}
:::

### [Fall Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1187581 .Subheading}

#### [Fall-protection Plan]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1187593 .Subheading}

- **[[12.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.06
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury due to
  falling in any of the circumstances described in paragraphs
  12.07(1)(a), (b) or (c) in a work place, the employer must, before any
  work activities begin,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} develop a fall-protection plan in consultation with
    the work place committee or the health and safety representative;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that a copy of the fall-protection plan is
    readily available at the work place for consultation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The fall-protection plan must specify

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the hazards that have been identified for each work
    area and each activity to be carried out at the work place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fall-protection systems that have been chosen
    to protect against the identified hazards;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a personal fall-protection system is used, the
    anchorage to be used during the work;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if a fall-arrest system is used, the clearance
    distance below each work area;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if there are no manufacturer's instructions with
    respect to the storage, maintenance, inspection, testing, fitting,
    installation, use or dismantling of equipment that is used in a
    fall-protection system and that is provided by an employer, the
    procedures to be followed for the purposes of paragraphs 12.05(1)(a)
    to (d); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the rescue procedures to be followed if a person
    falls.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The fall-protection plan must include, in annex, a
  copy of any manufacturer's instructions with respect to the storage,
  maintenance, inspection, testing, fitting, installation, use or
  dismantling of equipment that is used in a fall-protection system and
  that is provided by an employer.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The fall-protection systems referred to in paragraph
  (2)(b) are to be chosen in consultation with the work place committee
  or the health and safety representative, as appropriate for the work
  area and activity in question, taking into account the following order
  of priority:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} passive fall-protection system;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fall-restraint system;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} fall-arrest system; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} fall hazard zone system.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The clearance distance referred to in paragraph
  (2)(d) must be sufficient to prevent a person from hitting the ground
  or an object or surface below the work area during a fall.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187613){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187613.html"}
:::

#### [Fall-protection Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1187614 .Subheading}

- **[[12.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  employer must provide or put in place a fall-protection system if work
  is to be performed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from a structure or on a vehicle at a height of 3 m
    or more;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} from a ladder at a height of 3 m or more if,
    because of the nature of the work, the person performing it is
    unable to use at least one hand to hold onto the ladder; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at a height of less than 3 m if the surface onto
    which the person might fall would present a greater risk of injury
    than a solid, flat surface.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an employee is required to work on a vehicle and
  it is not feasible to provide or put in place a fall-protection
  system, an employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in consultation with the policy committee or, if
    there is no policy committee, the work place committee or the health
    and safety representative,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} perform a job safety analysis to eliminate or
      minimize the need for the employee to climb onto the vehicle or
      its load, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} provide every employee who could be required to
      climb onto the vehicle or its load with instruction and training
      by a qualified person on the safe method of climbing onto it and
      working there;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make a report in writing to the Head of Compliance
    and Enforcement setting out the reasons why it is not feasible to
    provide or put in place a fall-protection system and include in that
    report the job safety analysis and a description of the instruction
    and training referred to in paragraph (a); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide a copy of the report referred to in
    paragraph (b) to the policy committee or, if there is no policy
    committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
    representative.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The job safety analysis, instruction and training
  referred to in paragraph (2)(a) must be reviewed every two years in
  consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
  committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187628){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187628.html"}

Version](section-12.07-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 12.07"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[12.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.08
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A fall hazard zone must be at least
  2 m wide and extend the entire length of the unguarded edge.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A fall hazard zone must only be established on a
  surface that has a slope of five degrees or less.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person needs to carry out an activity in a fall
  hazard zone or cross a fall hazard zone to get to or from a work area,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer must ensure the presence of a fall
    hazard zone monitor whose exclusive duties are to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} supervise the fall hazard zone whenever an
      activity is being carried out there, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the requirements of the
      fall-protection plan are respected;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer must install, along the border between
    the fall hazard zone and any other work area, a raised demarcation
    line at a height of not less than 900 mm but not more than 1 100 mm;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employer must ensure that a personal
    fall-protection system is used.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a fall hazard zone system has been established in
  a work place, the employer must ensure that every person who is
  granted access to the work place is informed of the fall hazard zone
  system's existence and is familiar with the procedures to be followed
  for accessing, performing work in and leaving the fall hazard zone.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187640){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187640.html"}
:::

### [Protection Equipment and Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1187641 .Subheading}

#### [Fall Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1187670 .Subheading}

- **[[12.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.09
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury due to
  falling in a work place and the fall-protection plan requires that a
  personal fall-protection system be used, the employer must provide
  such a system to every person --- other than a person who is
  installing or dismantling a fall-protection system --- who is granted
  access to the work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A personal fall-protection system must meet the
  requirements set out in the following CSA Group standards:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Z259.16, Design of active fall-protection systems;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Z259.17, Selection and use of active
    fall-protection equipment and systems.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The components of a personal fall-protection system
  must meet the requirements set out in the following CSA Group
  standards:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Z259.1, Body belts and saddles for work positioning
    and travel restraint;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Z259.2.2, Self-retracting devices;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} Z259.2.3, Descent devices;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Z259.2.4, Fall arresters and vertical rigid rails;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} Z259.2.5, Fall arresters and vertical lifelines;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} Z259.10, Full body harnesses;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} Z259.11, Personal energy absorbers and lanyards;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} Z259.12, Connecting components for personal
    fall-arrest systems (PFAS);

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Z259.13, Manufactured horizontal lifeline systems;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} Z259.14, Fall restrict equipment for wood pole
    climbing; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} Z259.15, Anchorage connectors.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The components of a personal fall-protection system
  must be compatible and must be used in accordance with the
  manufacturer's instructions.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If more than one personal fall-protection system is
  secured to an anchorage, a separate anchorage connector must be used
  for each personal fall-protection system.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that a person who is using a
  personal fall-protection system wears and uses a full body harness.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that, before each work
  shift, every employee inspects their personal fall-protection system
  in accordance with the fall-protection plan.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that a person who works on
  an aerial device, boom-type elevating platform, scissor lift platform,
  forklift truck platform or any similar personnel lifting equipment in
  the circumstances described in subsection 12.07(1) uses a
  fall-restraint system that is connected to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an anchorage that is specified in the instructions
    of the manufacturer of the lifting equipment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if no anchorage is specified by the manufacturer,
    an anchorage that is certified by a person who is authorized to
    exercise the profession of engineering in Canada and that meets the
    requirements set out in CSA Group Standard Z259.16, Design of active
    fall-protection systems.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} If the use of a fall-restraint system would prevent
  the person referred to in subsection (8) from carrying out their work,
  the employer must ensure that a fall-arrest system is used.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187668){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187668.html"}
:::

#### [Protective Headwear]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895052 .Subheading}

- **[[12.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if there
  is a risk of head injury in a work place, the employer must ensure
  that protective headwear that meets the requirements set out in CSA
  Group Standard Z94.1, Industrial protective headwear --- Performance,
  selection, care, and use, or in ANSI Standard Z89.1, American National
  Standard for Industrial Head Protection, is worn.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the employer, in consultation with the policy
  committee or, if there is no policy committee, the work place
  committee or the health and safety representative, determines that the
  protective headwear referred to in subsection (1) does not eliminate
  or reduce the risk of injury, the employer must ensure that
  appropriate protective headwear selected by the employer, in
  consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
  committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative, is worn.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 44(F)
- SOR/95-533, s. 2(F)
- SOR/2002-208, s. 39
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187687){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187687.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protective Footwear]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895116 .Subheading}

- **[[12.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), if there
  is a risk of foot injury or electric shock in a work place, the
  employer must ensure that protective footwear that meets the
  requirements set out in CSA Group Standard Z195, Protective footwear
  is worn.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the employer, in consultation with the policy
  committee or, if there is no policy committee, the work place
  committee or the health and safety representative, determines that the
  protective footwear referred to in subsection (1) does not eliminate
  or reduce the risk of injury, the employer must ensure that
  appropriate protective footwear selected by them, in consultation with
  the policy committee or, if there is no policy committee, the work
  place committee or the health and safety representative, is worn.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury due to slipping in a
  work place, the employer must ensure that slip-resistant footwear is
  worn.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 51(F)
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187695){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187695.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.11"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Eye and Face Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895133 .Subheading}

- **[[12.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury to the
  eyes or face in a work place, the employer must provide every person
  who is granted access to the work place with eye or face protection
  equipment that is selected by the employer in accordance with Annex A
  of CSA Group Standard Z94.3, Eye and face protectors, and that meets
  the requirements set out in that standard.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is routine exposure to irritating airborne
  chemical agents, intense heat, liquid splashes, molten metals or
  similar agents in a work place, contact lenses must not be worn.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-208, s. 27
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187702){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187702.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.12"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Respiratory Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895137 .Subheading}

- **[[12.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury or
  disease due to exposure to an oxygen deficient atmosphere in a work
  place, the employer must provide every person who is granted access to
  the work place with respiratory protective equipment by means of which
  air will be supplied and that meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is listed in the United States National
    Institute for Occupational Safety and Health publication entitled
    Certified Equipment List, as amended from time to time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it meets the requirements set out in CSA Standard
    Z94.4, Selection, use, and care of respirators; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it protects the respiratory tract against the
    oxygen deficiency.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2021-140, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury or disease due to
  exposure to an airborne hazardous substance, other than a CBRN agent,
  in a work place, the employer must provide every person who is granted
  access to the work place with respiratory protective equipment that
  meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if air is supplied by means of the respiratory
    protective equipment, it is listed in the United States National
    Institute for Occupational Safety and Health publication entitled
    Certified Equipment List, as amended from time to time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if air is not supplied by means of the respiratory
    protective equipment, it is listed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the United States National Institute for
      Occupational Safety and Health publication entitled Certified
      Equipment List, as amended from time to time, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the CSA Group publication entitled CSA Group
      Product Listing, as amended from time to time;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for respiratory protective equipment referred to in
    subparagraph (b)(ii), it meets the requirements set out in CSA
    Standard Z94.4.1, Performance of filtering respirators;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it meets the requirements set out in CSA Standard
    Z94.4, Selection, use, and care of respirators; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} it protects the respiratory tract against the
    hazardous substance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury or disease due to
  exposure to CBRN agents in a work place, the employer must provide
  every person who is granted access to the work place with respiratory
  protection equipment for protection against those CBRN agents that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a first responder in respect of
    events that may involve CBRN agents, meets the requirements set out
    in CSA Standard Z1610, Protection of first responders from chemical,
    biological, radiological, and nuclear (CBRN) events; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is listed in the United States National Institute
      for Occupational Safety and Health publication entitled Certified
      Equipment List, as amended from time to time, and meets the
      requirements set out in CSA Standard Z94.4, Selection, use, and
      care of respirators, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements set out in CSA Standard
      Z1610, Protection of first responders from chemical, biological,
      radiological, and nuclear (CBRN) events.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that respiratory protective
  equipment referred to in subsections (1) and (2) and subparagraph
  (3)(b)(i) is used and cared for in accordance with CSA Standard Z94.4,
  Selection, use, and care of respirators.

::: HistoricalNote

**[[12.13.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.13.1 .sectionLabel}** The employer
must ensure that air that is supplied by means of respiratory protective
equipment referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (2)(a), and the system that
supplies the air, including its cylinders, meet the requirements set out
in CSA Standard Z180.1, Compressed breathing air and systems.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 4](#1343799-1344382){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343799-1344382.html"}
:::

#### [Skin Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895144 .Subheading}

- **[[12.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of injury or
  disease to or transmitted through the skin in a work place, the
  employer must provide every person who is granted access to the work
  place with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a shield or screen;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a cream or other product to be applied to the skin;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} appropriate protective clothing.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If sunscreen is provided by the employer, the
  sunscreen must be broad-spectrum and have a minimum sun protection
  factor of 30.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 53(F)
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187727){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187727.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.14-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.14"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protection Against Drowning]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895154 .Subheading}

- **[[12.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of drowning in a
  work place, the employer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide every person who is granted access to the
    work place with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a life jacket that meets the requirements set out
      in the Canadian General Standards Board Standard 65.7, Life
      Jackets, in its 2007 version, as it existed before its withdrawal
      in November 2016, published on the Government of Canada website
      publications.gc.ca,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a personal flotation device or buoyancy aid that
      has a level 70 performance within the meaning of UL Standard
      12402-5, Personal flotation devices --- Part 5: Buoyancy aids
      (level 50) --- Safety requirements, when tested in accordance with
      UL Standard 12402-9, Personal Flotation Devices --- Part 9: Test
      Methods, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a safety net or a personal fall-protection
      system;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide emergency equipment and ensure that it is
    in good operating condition and readily available;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that a qualified person is available on site
    to operate the emergency equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if appropriate, provide a power boat and ensure
    that it is in good operating condition and readily available; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} prepare written emergency procedures that contain

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a full description of the procedures to be
      followed and the responsibilities of all persons who are granted
      access to the work place, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the location of any emergency equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the work place is a wharf, dock, pier, quay or
  similar structure, a ladder that extends at least two rungs below
  water level must be installed on the face of the structure every 60 m
  along its length.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187743){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187743.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.15-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.15"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Loose Clothing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895162 .Subheading}

**[[12.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.16 .sectionLabel}** If there is a risk
of injury in a work place that is due to loose clothing, long hair,
jewellery or similar items, the employer must ensure that, if those
items are worn, they are tied, covered or otherwise secured in order to
eliminate or reduce the risk of injury.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187748){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187748.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.16-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.16"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protection Against Moving Vehicles]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1187749 .Subheading}

**[[12.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.17 .sectionLabel}** If there is a risk
of injury in a work place that is due to moving vehicles, the employer
must provide every person who is granted access to the work place with
high-visibility safety apparel that meets the requirements set out in
CSA Group Standard Z96, High-visibility safety apparel.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187753){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187753.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.17"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Defective Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1187756 .Subheading}

**[[12.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.18 .sectionLabel}** If an employee
finds a defect in protection equipment that may render it unsafe, they
must report the defect to their employer as soon as feasible.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187759){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187759.html"}
:::

- **[[12.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer must remove from service
  any protection equipment that has a defect that may render it unsafe
  and must mark or tag that equipment to indicate that it is unsafe for
  use.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Protection equipment that has a defect that renders
  it unsafe may only be returned to service if it is restored to good
  operating condition by a qualified person in accordance with section
  12.05.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187764){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187764.html"}
:::

### [Instruction and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1187766 .Subheading}

- **[[12.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer must ensure that every person who is granted access to a work
  place and who uses protection equipment is provided with instruction
  by a qualified person in the use of that equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that, in addition to the
  instruction referred to in subsection (1), every employee who uses
  protection equipment is provided with instruction and training by a
  qualified person in the operation and maintenance of the equipment and
  training by a qualified person in its use.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a fall-protection plan has been developed for a
  work place under paragraph 12.06(1)(a), the employer must ensure that
  every employee is provided with training by a qualified person in
  respect of the fall-protection plan.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk of drowning in a work place, the
  employer must ensure that every person who is granted access to the
  work place is provided with instruction by a qualified person in
  respect of the written emergency procedures referred to in paragraph
  12.15(1)(e).

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that the instruction and
  training referred to in subsections (1) to (4) is summarized in
  writing and must keep a copy of that summary readily available for
  consultation by every person who is granted access to the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187774){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187774.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.2-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1187775 .Subheading}

- **[[12.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer must keep a record of
  all of the protection equipment that is provided by them other than
  disposable equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The record must contain the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the equipment and the date of its
    acquisition by the employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and result of each inspection and test of
    the equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date and nature of any maintenance work
    performed on the equipment since its acquisition by the employer;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person who performed the
    inspection, test or maintenance of the equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The record must be kept at the work place where the
  protection equipment is located and must continue to be kept there for
  a period of two years beginning on the day on which the equipment is
  permanently removed from service or, if a standard referred to in this
  Part requires that records be kept in respect of that equipment for a
  longer period, for that longer period.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-243, s. 4](#1175887-1187785){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1175887-1187785.html"}
:::

**[[12.3 to 12.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.3_to_12.9
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/2019-243, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

## [PART XIII]{.HLabel1}[Tools and Machinery]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895165 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895166 .Subheading}

**[[13.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[explosive actuated fastening tool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a tool
that, by means of an explosive force, propels or discharges a fastener
for the purpose of impinging it on, affixing it to or causing it to
penetrate another object or material. ([pistolet de scellement à
cartouches explosives]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [Design, Construction, Operation and Use of Tools]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895168 .Subheading}

**[[13.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.2 .sectionLabel}** The exterior surface
of any tool used by an employee in a fire hazard area shall be made of
non-sparking material.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 54
:::

**[[13.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.3 .sectionLabel}** All portable electric
tools used by employees shall meet the standards set out in CSA Standard
CAN C22.2 No. 71.1-M89, Portable Electric Tools, the English version of
which is dated September 1989 and the French version of which is dated
February 1991.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 48
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344395){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344395.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.3"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[13.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), all
  portable electric tools used by employees shall be grounded.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to tools that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are powered by a self-contained battery;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have a protective system of double insulation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are used in a location where reliable grounding
    cannot be obtained if the tools are supplied from a double insulated
    portable ground fault circuit interrupter of the class A type that
    meets the standards set out in CSA Standard C22.2 No. 144-1977,
    Ground Fault Circuit Interrupters, dated March, 1977.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 49(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 83(F)](#1177549-1188141){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177549-1188141.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344396){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344396.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.4-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 13.4"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.5 .sectionLabel}** All portable electric
tools used by employees in a fire hazard area shall be marked as
appropriate for use or designed for use in the area of that hazard.

**[[13.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.6 .sectionLabel}** Where an air hose is
connected to a portable air-powered tool used by an employee, a
restraining device shall be attached

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where an employee may be injured by the tool falling,
  to the tool; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to all hose connections, in order to prevent injury
  to an employee in the event of an accidental disconnection of a hose.

<!-- -->

- **[[13.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All explosive actuated fastening
  tools used by employees shall meet the standards set out in CSA
  Standard Z166-1975, Explosive Actuated Fastening Tools, dated June,
  1975.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall operate an explosive actuated
  fastening tool unless authorized to do so by his employer.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who operates an explosive actuated
  fastening tool shall operate it in accordance with the CSA Standard
  referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344398){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344398.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.7"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.8 .sectionLabel}** All chain saws used
by employees shall meet the standards set out in CSA Standard
CAN3-Z62.1-M85, Chain Saws, dated February, 1985.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344400){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344400.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.8"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Defective Tools and Machines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895192 .Subheading}

**[[13.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.9 .sectionLabel}** If an employee finds
any defect in a tool or machine that may render it unsafe for use, they
shall report the defect to their employer as soon as feasible.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 84](#1177551-1188144){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177551-1188144.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.9"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.10 .sectionLabel}** An employer shall
mark or tag as unsafe and remove from service any tool or machine used
by his employees that has a defect that may render it unsafe for use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 85(F)](#1177553-1188146){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177553-1188146.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.10-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.10"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Instructions and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895195 .Subheading}

**[[13.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.11 .sectionLabel}** Every employee
shall be instructed and trained by a qualified person appointed by his
employer in the safe and proper inspection, maintenance and use of all
tools and machinery that he is required to use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 85(F)](#1177553-1188148){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177553-1188148.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.11"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[13.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall maintain a
  manual of operating instructions for each type of portable electric
  tool, portable air-powered tool, explosive actuated fastening tool and
  machine used by his employees.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A manual referred to in subsection (1) shall be kept
  by the employer readily available for examination by an employee who
  is required to use the tool or machine to which the manual applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 55(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 86(F)](#1177555-1188149){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177555-1188149.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.12"){.PITLink}
:::

### [General Requirements for Machine Guards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895202 .Subheading}

- **[[13.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every machine that has exposed
  moving, rotating, electrically charged or hot parts or that processes,
  transports or handles material that constitutes a hazard to an
  employee shall be equipped with a machine guard that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents the employee or any part of his body from
    coming into contact with the parts or material;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevents access by the employee to the area of
    exposure to the hazard during the operation of the machine; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} makes the machine inoperative if the employee or
    any part of his clothing is in or near a part of the machine that is
    likely to cause injury.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If feasible, a machine guard referred to in
  subsection (1) shall not be removable.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A machine guard shall be so constructed, installed
  and maintained that it meets the requirements of subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 50(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 87](#1177557-1188150){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177557-1188150.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.13-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.13"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Use, Operation, Repair and Maintenance of Machine Guards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895212 .Subheading}

**[[13.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.14 .sectionLabel}** Machine guards
shall be operated, maintained and repaired by a qualified person.

**[[13.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.15 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section
13.16, where a machine guard is installed on a machine, no person shall
use or operate the machine unless the machine guard is in its proper
position.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 51
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 7(F)](#1299277-1300252){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299277-1300252.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.15-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.15"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[13.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where it
  is necessary to remove a machine guard from a machine in order to
  perform repair or maintenance work on the machine, no person shall
  perform the repair or maintenance work unless the machine has been
  locked out in accordance with a written lock out procedure provided by
  the employer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to lock out a machine referred
  to in subsection (1) in order to perform repair or maintenance work on
  the machine, the work may be performed if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person performing the work follows written
    instructions provided by the employer that will ensure that any
    hazard to that person is not significantly greater than it would be
    if the machine had been locked out; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person performing the work

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} obtains a written authorization from the employer
      each time the work is performed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} performs the work under the direct supervision
      of a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 88](#1177562-1188153){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177562-1188153.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.16-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.16"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.17 .sectionLabel}** A copy of the
instructions referred to in section 13.16 shall be kept readily
available by the employer for the information of persons who perform
repair and maintenance work on his machines.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 89(F)](#1177569-1188156){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177569-1188156.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.17"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Abrasive Wheels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895225 .Subheading}

**[[13.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.18 .sectionLabel}** Abrasive wheels
shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} used only on machines equipped with machine guards,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} mounted between flanges, and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} operated

in accordance with sections 4 to 6 of CSA Standard B173.5-1979, Safety
Requirements for the Use, Care and Protection of Abrasive Wheels, dated
February, 1979.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 56(F)
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344401){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344401.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.18-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.18"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.19 .sectionLabel}** A bench grinder
shall be equipped with a work rest or other device that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents the work piece from jamming between the
  abrasive wheel and the wheel guard; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not make contact with the abrasive wheel at any
  time.

### [Mechanical Power Transmission Apparatus]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895236 .Subheading}

**[[13.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.20 .sectionLabel}** Equipment used in
the mechanical transmission of power shall be guarded in accordance with
sections 7 to 10 of ANSI Standard ANSI B15.1-1972, Safety Standard for
Mechanical Power Transmission Apparatus, dated July, 1972.

### [Woodworking Machinery]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895238 .Subheading}

**[[13.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.21 .sectionLabel}** Woodworking
machinery shall be guarded in accordance with clause 3.3 of CSA Standard
Z114-M1977, Safety Code for the Woodworking Industry, dated March, 1977.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344403){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344403.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.21-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.21"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Punch Presses]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895240 .Subheading}

**[[13.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.22 .sectionLabel}** Punch presses shall
meet the standards set out in CSA Standard Z142-1976, Code for the
Guarding of Punch Presses at Point of Operation, dated February, 1976.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344405){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344405.html"}
:::
