[Previous
Version](section-13.22-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.22"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART XIV]{.HLabel1}[Materials Handling]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895242 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895243 .Subheading}

**[[14.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[materials handling equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[materials handling equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    equipment, including its supporting structures, auxiliary equipment
    and rigging devices, used to transport, lift, move or position
    persons, materials, goods or things and includes mobile equipment
    used to lift, hoist or position persons, but does not include an
    elevating device that is permanently installed in a building;
    ([appareil de manutention]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[motorized hand-rider truck]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[motorized hand-rider truck]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means motorized
    materials handling equipment that is designed to be controlled by a
    walking or onboard operator; ([chariot à conducteur porté ou
    accompagnant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who controls the
    operation of motorized or manual, materials handling equipment and
    who has received or is receiving instructions and training in
    respect of the procedures referred to in subsection 14.23(1) or (3)
    as the case may be; ([opérateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[safe working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[safe working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the maximum load
    that the motorized or manual materials handling equipment is
    designed and constructed to handle or support safely under
    particular operating conditions; ([charge de travail
    admissible]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[signaller]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[signaller]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person instructed by an
    employer to direct, by means of visual or auditory signals, the safe
    movement and operation of motorized materials handling equipment.
    ([signaleur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 90](#1177571-1188157){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177571-1188157.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895252 .Subheading}

**[[14.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply in respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 14.4(4), the use and operation
  of motor vehicles on public roads;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the use and operation of tackle regulated pursuant to
  the [Canada Shipping Act](/eng/acts/S-9) in the loading or unloading
  of ships; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the underground workings of mines.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[Design and Construction]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895259 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895260 .Subheading}

- **[[14.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized and manual materials
  handling equipment shall, if feasible, be so designed and constructed
  that if there is a failure of any part of the materials handling
  equipment, that failure will not result in loss of control of the
  materials handling equipment or create a hazardous condition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All glass and other transparent materials used in
  doors, windows and other parts of motorized materials handling
  equipment shall be of a type that does not shatter into sharp or
  dangerous pieces on impact.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 14.51(1), an employer shall
  ensure that the onboard operator's compartment in, or position on,
  motorized materials handling equipment provides adequate adjustability
  of range to accommodate properly the operator for the work that is
  required to be done.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 91](#1177573-1188159){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177573-1188159.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.3"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protection from Falling Objects]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895267 .Subheading}

- **[[14.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  motorized materials handling equipment is used under such
  circumstances that there is a risk that the on board operator of the
  equipment could be struck by a falling object or shifting load, the
  employer shall equip the motorized materials handling equipment with a
  protective structure of such a design, construction and strength that
  it will, under all foreseeable conditions, prevent the penetration of
  the object or load into the compartment or position occupied by the
  operator.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a motorized
  hand-rider truck unless there is a risk of its operator being struck
  by a falling object or shifting load.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A protective structure referred to in subsection (1)
  shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} constructed of non-combustible or fire-resistant
    material; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designed to permit quick exit from the motorized
    materials handling equipment in an emergency.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If there is a risk that materials, goods or things
  could shift and endanger employees in a motor vehicle acquired after
  July 1, 1995 and having a gross vehicle weight of less than 4 500 kg,
  the employer shall install a bulkhead or other means to protect the
  employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 92](#1177575-1188161){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177575-1188161.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.4"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[14.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.5 .sectionLabel}** Where during the
loading or unloading of motorized materials handling equipment the load
will pass over the operator's compartment or position, the operator
shall not occupy that compartment in, or position on, the equipment
unless it is equipped with a protective structure required under section
14.4.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Protection from Overturning]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895280 .Subheading}

- **[[14.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 14.51(2),
  where motorized materials handling equipment is used in circumstances
  where it may turn over, it shall be fitted with a rollover protection
  device that meets the standards set out in CSA Standard B352-M1980,
  Rollover Protective Structures (ROPS) for Agricultural, Construction,
  Earthmoving, Forestry, Industrial, and Mining Machines, the English
  version of which is dated September 1980 and the French version of
  which is dated April 1991, as amended from time to time, and that will
  prevent the operator of the motorized materials handling equipment
  from being trapped or crushed under the equipment if it does turn
  over.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 14.51(1), all motorized
  materials handling equipment used in circumstances described in
  subsection (1) shall be fitted with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} seat-belts; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} restraining devices preventing the displacement of
    the battery if the equipment turns over.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 57(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 52
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 93(F)](#1177580-1188165){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177580-1188165.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344406){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344406.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.6-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 14.6"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Seat-Belts]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895288 .Subheading}

**[[14.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.7 .sectionLabel}** Where motorized
materials handling equipment is used under conditions where a seat-belt
or shoulder-type strap restraining device is likely to contribute to the
safety of the operator or passengers, the materials handling equipment
shall be equipped with such a belt or device.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/94-263, s. 53(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 94(F)](#1177582-1188166){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177582-1188166.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.7"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fuel Tanks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895292 .Subheading}

- **[[14.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a fuel tank, compressed gas cylinder or other container or any
  associated assembly mounted on motorized materials handling equipment
  contains a hazardous substance, the employer shall ensure that the
  tank, cylinder, container or assembly is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} so located or guarded that under all conditions it
    is not hazardous to the health or safety of an employee who is
    required to operate or ride on the materials handling equipment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} connected to fuel overflow and vent pipes that are
    so located that fuel spills and vapours cannot be

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} ignited by hot exhaust pipes or other hot or
      sparking parts, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} hazardous to the health or safety of an employee
      who is required to operate or ride on the materials handling
      equipment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} labelled on its servicing caps or covers as to its
    contents.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 14.51(2), the installation,
  operation and maintenance of propane fuelled motorized materials
  handling equipment shall be in accordance with the Canadian Gas
  Association Standard CAN/CGA-B149.2-M91, Propane Installation Code,
  dated 1991, as amended from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 28
:::

#### [Protection from Elements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895303 .Subheading}

- **[[14.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized materials handling
  equipment that is regularly used outdoors shall be fitted with a roof
  or other structure that will protect the operator from exposure to any
  weather condition that is likely to be hazardous to the operator's
  health or safety.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the heat produced by motorized materials
  handling equipment results in a temperature above 26°C in the
  compartment or position occupied by that operator, the area shall be
  protected from the heat by an insulated barrier.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 41
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 95(F)](#1177584-1188167){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177584-1188167.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.9"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Vibration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895309 .Subheading}

**[[14.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.10 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
subsection 14.51(1), an employer shall ensure that all motorized
materials handling equipment in use is designed and constructed so that
any employee required to operate or ride on it will not be injured or
its control will not be impaired by any vibration, jolting or uneven
movement of the materials handling equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 58
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Controls]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895313 .Subheading}

**[[14.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.11 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
subsection 14.51(1), the design and arrangement of displays and controls
and the design and layout of the operator's compartment or position on
all motorized materials handling equipment shall not hinder or prevent
its operator from operating the equipment and shall, if feasible,
maximize its operator's ability to collect, comprehend and process
information necessary for the safe use of the equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 96](#1177586-1188168){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177586-1188168.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.11"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fire Extinguishers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895317 .Subheading}

- **[[14.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall equip motorized
  materials handling equipment that is used for transporting or handling
  flammable substances with a dry chemical fire extinguisher.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A fire extinguisher referred to in subsection (1)
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have not less than a 5 B, C rating as defined in
    the National Fire Code;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meet the standards set out in section 6.2 of the
    National Fire Code; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be so located that it is readily accessible to the
    operator of the motorized materials handling equipment while the
    operator is in the operating position.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to motorized materials
  handling equipment that is used exclusively within a building that has
  the fire extinguishers required by Part XVII.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Means of Entering and Exiting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895327 .Subheading}

- **[[14.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 14.51(1), an
  employer shall ensure that all motorized materials handling equipment
  has a safe means of entering and exiting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the work area of the operator; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other place on the equipment to which an
    employee requires regular access.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A safe means referred to in subsection (1) shall take
  into account the employee's body dimensions while wearing personal
  protective equipment and shall not require the employee to jump from
  the motorized materials handling equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 97(F)](#1177588-1188170){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177588-1188170.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.13-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.13"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Lighting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895335 .Subheading}

- **[[14.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where
  motorized materials handling equipment is used by an employee in a
  work place at night or at any time when the level of lighting within
  the work place is less than 10 lx, the materials handling equipment
  shall be fitted with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} warning lights on the front and rear that are
    visible from a distance of not less than 100 m; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} lighting that ensures the safe operation of the
    equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No motorized materials handling equipment shall be
  used at night on a route that is used by other vehicles unless it is
  fitted with such lights as are required under the laws of the province
  in which the equipment is used.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Control Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895343 .Subheading}

**[[14.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.15 .sectionLabel}** All motorized
materials handling equipment shall be fitted with braking, steering and
other control systems that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} are capable of controlling and stopping its movement
  and that of any hoist, bucket or other part of the equipment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respond reliably and quickly to moderate effort on
  the part of the employee controlling them.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Warning Devices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895349 .Subheading}

- **[[14.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized materials handling
  equipment that is used in an area occupied by employees and that
  travels

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} forward at speeds in excess of 8 km/h shall be
    fitted with a horn or other similar audible warning device; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in reverse shall, subject to subsection 14.51(1),
    be fitted with a horn or other similar audible warning device that
    automatically operates while it travels in reverse.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an audible warning device referred to in
  subsection (1) cannot be clearly heard above the noise of the
  motorized materials handling equipment and any surrounding noise, does
  not allow enough time for a person to avoid the danger in question or
  does not otherwise provide adequate warning, other visual, audible or
  tactile warning devices or methods shall be used so that adequate
  warning is provided.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the use of an audible warning device referred
  to in subsection (1) would result in night-time noise levels in excess
  of those allowed by a municipal by-law applicable where the motorized
  material handling equipment is used, visual or tactile warning devices
  or methods may be used at night if such devices or methods provide an
  adequate warning.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Rear View Mirrors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895358 .Subheading}

**[[14.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.17 .sectionLabel}** Where motorized
materials handling equipment cannot be operated safely in reverse unless
it is fitted with sufficient rear-view mirrors, it shall be so fitted.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 59(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Guided Industrial Vehicles]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895362 .Subheading}

**[[14.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.18 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
subsection 14.51(2), the design, construction, operation and maintenance
of guided industrial vehicles shall meet the standards set out in the
American Society of Mechanical Engineers Standard ASME B56.5-1993,
Safety Standard for Guided Industrial Vehicles and Automated Functions
of Manned Industrial Vehicles, dated 1993, as amended from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Conveyors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895366 .Subheading}

**[[14.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.19 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
subsection 14.51(2), the design, construction, operation and maintenance
of each conveyor, cableway or other similar motorized materials handling
equipment shall meet the standards set out in the American Society of
Mechanical Engineers Standard ASME B20.1-1993, Safety Standard for
Conveyors and Related Equipment, dated 1993, as amended from time to
time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Maintenance, Use and Operation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895370 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2021-122, s. 8(F)

\]
:::

#### [Inspection, Testing and Maintenance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895371 .Subheading}

- **[[14.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.20
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before motorized or manual materials
  handling equipment is used for the first time in a work place, the
  employer shall set out in writing instructions on the inspection,
  testing and maintenance of that materials handling equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall
  specify the nature and frequency of inspections, testing and
  maintenance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The inspection, testing and maintenance referred to
  in subsection (1) shall be performed by a qualified person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} complies with the instructions referred to in that
    subsection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} makes and signs a report of each inspection, test
    or maintenance work performed by the qualified person.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A report referred to in paragraph (3)(b) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include the date of the inspection, testing or
    maintenance performed by the qualified person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the materials handling equipment that was
    inspected, tested or maintained; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the safety observations of the qualified
    person.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep at the work place at which
  the motorized or manual materials handling equipment is located a copy
  of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the instructions referred to in subsection (1) for
    as long as the materials handling equipment is in use; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the report referred to in paragraph (3)(b) for a
    period of one year after the report is signed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 98(F)](#1177590-1188171){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177590-1188171.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.20-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.20"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Mobile Cranes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895387 .Subheading}

**[[14.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.21 .sectionLabel}** Mobile cranes shall
be inspected, tested and maintained in accordance with the requirements
of section 5 of CSA Standard Z150-1974, Safety Code for Mobile Cranes,
dated 1974, and its supplement Z150S1-1977 entitled Supplement 1-1977 to
CSA Standard Z150-1974 Safety Code for Mobile Cranes, published in 1977.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 60(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344407){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344407.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.21-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.21"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Split Rim Wheels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895391 .Subheading}

- **[[14.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer whose employees
  maintain or repair motorized materials handling equipment equipped
  with split rim wheels shall set out in writing instructions for those
  employees on the maintenance and repair of those wheels.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall
  include instructions on training, inspection, installation, guarding,
  compatibility of parts used and repairs relating to the assembling and
  disassembling of split rim wheels.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep a copy of the instructions
  referred to in subsection (1) at the work place in which the motorized
  materials handling equipment referred to in that subsection is kept
  for as long as the equipment is in use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 61(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 99(F)](#1177597-1188172){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177597-1188172.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.22-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.22"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Instruction and Training]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895398 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 100(F)

\]
:::

- **[[14.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  employer shall ensure that every operator of motorized materials
  handling equipment has been instructed and trained in the procedures
  to be followed for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its inspection;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its fuelling; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} its safe and proper use, in accordance with any
    instructions provided by the manufacturer and taking into account
    the conditions of the work place in which the operator will operate
    the materials handling equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an employer with
  respect to an operator who, under the direct supervision of a
  qualified person, is being instructed and trained in the use of
  motorized materials handling equipment or in the procedures referred
  to in that subsection.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall ensure that every operator of
  manual materials handling equipment receives on-the-job training by a
  qualified person on the procedures to be followed for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its inspection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its safe and proper use, in accordance with any
    instructions of the manufacturer and taking into account the
    conditions of the work place in which the operator will operate the
    manual materials handling equipment and the operator's physical
    capabilities.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall keep a written record, in
  respect of an operator, of any instruction or training referred to in
  subsection (1) for as long as the operator remains in the employer's
  employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 101(F)](#1177601-1188176){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177601-1188176.html"}
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 9](#1299281-1300257){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299281-1300257.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.23-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 14.23"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Qualifications]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895411 .Subheading}

**[[14.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.24 .sectionLabel}** No employer shall
require an employee to operate motorized or manual materials handling
equipment unless the employee

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is an operator; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the laws of the province in which the equipment
  is operated require an operator's licence, possesses an operator's
  licence issued by any province.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 62(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Signals]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895417 .Subheading}

**[[14.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.25 .sectionLabel}** No employer shall
require an operator to operate motorized materials handling equipment
unless the operator

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is directed by a signaller; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} has an unobstructed view of the area in which the
  equipment is to be operated.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 63
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2009-147, s. 8(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.25-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.25"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[14.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer who wishes to use
  signals to direct the movement of motorized materials handling
  equipment shall establish a single code of signals to be used by
  signallers in all of the employer's work places.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), signals from the code
  referred to in subsection (1) shall be given by a signaller, who may
  use only those signals.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A signal to stop given by any person granted access
  to the work place by the employer shall be obeyed by an operator.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} No signaller shall perform duties other than
  signalling while the motorized materials handling equipment under the
  signaller's direction is in operation.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where any movement of motorized materials handling
  equipment that is directed by a signaller poses a risk to the safety
  of any person, the signaller shall not give the signal to move until
  that person is warned of, or protected from, the risk.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where the operator of any motorized materials
  handling equipment does not understand a signal, the operator shall
  consider that signal to be a stop signal.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2021-122, s. 10](#1299283-1300259){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1299283-1300259.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.26-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.26"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[14.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where the
  use by a signaller of visual signals will not be an effective means of
  communication, the employer shall provide the signaller and the
  operator with a telephone, radio or other audible signalling device.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No radio transmitting equipment shall be used in a
  work place for the transmission of signals where such use may activate
  electric blasting equipment in that place.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a signalling device referred to in
  subsection (1) functions unreliably or improperly and the operation of
  any motorized materials handling equipment cannot be safely directed
  by another means of signalling, use of the motorized materials
  handling equipment shall be discontinued until the signalling device
  is repaired or replaced.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If an employee finds any defect in radio transmitting
  signalling equipment that may render it unsafe for use, they shall
  report the defect to their employer as soon as feasible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 54(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 102](#1177608-1188177){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177608-1188177.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.27-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.27"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Gradients]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895439 .Subheading}

**[[14.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.28 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall
operate and no employer shall permit an employee to operate motorized
materials handling equipment on a ramp with a gradient in excess of the
lesser of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the gradient that is recommended as safe by the
  manufacturer of the motorized materials handling equipment, either
  loaded or unloaded, as applicable; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} such gradient as a qualified person determines to be
  safe, having regard to the mechanical condition of the motorized
  materials handling equipment and its load and traction.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Repairs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895445 .Subheading}

- **[[14.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.29
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized or manual materials
  handling equipment that creates a health or safety hazard owing to a
  defect in the materials handling equipment shall be taken out of
  service until it has been repaired or modified by a qualified person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), any repair, modification
  or replacement of a part of any motorized or manual materials handling
  equipment shall at least maintain the safety factor of the materials
  handling equipment or part.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a part of less strength or quality than the
  original part is used in the repair, modification or replacement of a
  part of any motorized or manual materials handling equipment, the
  employer shall restrict the use of the materials handling equipment to
  such loading and use as will ensure the retention of the original
  safety factor of the equipment or part.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall keep a record of any repair or
  modification referred to in subsection (1) and of any restriction on
  use imposed pursuant to subsection (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, art. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 39
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 103(F)](#1177613-1188179){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177613-1188179.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.29-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.29"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Transporting and Positioning Employees]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895453 .Subheading}

- **[[14.30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.30
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized or manual materials
  handling equipment shall not be used for transporting an employee and
  no employee shall so use the equipment unless the equipment is
  specifically designed for that purpose.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Motorized or manual materials handling equipment
  shall not be used for hoisting or positioning an employee, unless the
  equipment is equipped with a platform, bucket or basket designed for
  those purposes.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any motorized materials handling equipment that is
  normally used for transporting employees from place to place in a work
  place shall be equipped with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a mechanical parking brake; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hydraulic or pneumatic braking system.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Loading, Unloading and Maintenance While in Motion]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895462 .Subheading}

**[[14.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.31 .sectionLabel}** No materials, goods
or things shall be picked up from, or placed on, any motorized or manual
materials handling equipment while the equipment is in motion unless the
equipment is specifically designed for that purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

**[[14.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.32 .sectionLabel}** Except in the case
of an emergency, no employee shall get on or off of motorized or manual
materials handling equipment while it is in motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

- **[[14.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no
  repair, maintenance or cleaning work shall be performed on motorized
  or manual materials handling equipment while the materials handling
  equipment is in use.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Fixed parts of motorized or manual materials handling
  equipment may be repaired, maintained or cleaned while the materials
  handling equipment is being used if the parts are so isolated or
  guarded that the use of the materials handling equipment does not
  present a risk to the safety of the employee performing the repair,
  maintenance or cleaning work.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 64(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 55(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Positioning the Load]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895474 .Subheading}

- **[[14.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If motorized or manual materials
  handling equipment is travelling with a raised or suspended load, its
  operator shall ensure that the load is carried as close as possible to
  the ground or floor and shall not in any case transport the load at or
  beyond the point at which the loaded equipment becomes unstable.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any load, other than bulk materials, that would
  likely slide on or fall from motorized or manual materials handling
  equipment resulting in a hazardous condition shall be secured to
  prevent such movement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 104](#1177615-1188180){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177615-1188180.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.34-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.34"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Tools]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895480 .Subheading}

**[[14.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.35 .sectionLabel}** Tools, tool boxes
or spare parts that are carried on motorized or manual materials
handling equipment shall be securely stored.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Housekeeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895484 .Subheading}

**[[14.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.36 .sectionLabel}** The floor, cab and
other occupied parts of motorized materials handling equipment shall be
kept free of any grease, oil, materials, tools, equipment or other
hazards that may cause an employee to slip or trip or may create a fire
hazard or otherwise interfere with the safe operation of the equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 65(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Parking]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895488 .Subheading}

- **[[14.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No motorized or manual materials
  handling equipment shall be parked in a corridor, aisle, doorway or
  other place where it may interfere with the safe movement of persons,
  materials, goods or things.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where motorized or manual materials handling
  equipment is required to enter or exit a vehicle other than a railway
  car to load or unload materials, goods or things to or from the
  vehicle, the vehicle shall be immobilized and secured against
  accidental movement, by means additional to the vehicle's braking
  system.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where motorized or manual materials handling
  equipment is required to enter or exit a railway car to load or unload
  materials, goods or things to or from the railway car, the railway car
  shall be immobilized.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any motorized materials handling equipment that is
  left unattended shall be immobilized against accidental movement, by
  applying a parking brake or other braking device.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 66(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Materials Handling Area]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895496 .Subheading}

- **[[14.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[materials
  handling area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area within which
  materials handling equipment may create a hazard to any person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall cause warning signs to be posted,
  or a signaller to be in control, at the approaches to any materials
  handling area while materials handling operations are in progress.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Only the following persons may enter a materials
  handling area while materials handling operations are in progress:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Head of Compliance and Enforcement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an employee whose presence in the materials
    handling area is essential to the conduct, supervision or safety of
    the materials handling operations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who has been authorized by the employer to
    be in the materials handling area while materials handling
    operations are in progress.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If any person other than a person referred to in
  subsection (3) enters a materials handling area, the employer shall
  cause the materials handling operations in the immediate vicinity of
  the unauthorized person to be immediately discontinued and to remain
  discontinued until the person has left that materials handling area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/88-632, s. 67(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 65(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 38
- SOR/2014-148, s. 7
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312229){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312229.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.38-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 14.38"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Hazard Areas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895507 .Subheading}

- **[[14.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.39
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no
  motorized or manual materials handling equipment shall be used in an
  area in which it may contact an electrical cable, a pipeline
  containing a hazardous substance or any other hazard known to the
  employer, unless the employer has informed the operator of the
  presence and location of the hazard and of the safety clearance that
  the operator must maintain with respect to the hazard.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer is unable to determine with
  reasonable certainty the location of an electrical cable or a pipeline
  containing a hazardous substance, the electrical cable shall be
  de-energized or the pipeline shut down and drained before any activity
  involving the use of motorized materials handling equipment commences
  within the area of possible contact with the electrical cable or the
  pipeline.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 43(F)
:::

#### [Rear Dumping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895513 .Subheading}

**[[14.40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.40 .sectionLabel}** If rear-dumping
motorized materials handling equipment is used to discharge a load at
the edge of a sudden drop in grade level that may cause the equipment to
tip and in order to prevent the motorized materials handling equipment
from tipping,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a bumping block shall be used; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a signaller shall give directions to the operator of
  the equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2009-84, s. 1(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 105](#1177617-1188182){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177617-1188182.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.40-20090305.html "Link to previous version of section 14.40"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fuelling]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895519 .Subheading}

**[[14.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.41 .sectionLabel}** Where motorized
materials handling equipment is fuelled in a work place, a qualified
person shall do the fuelling in accordance with procedures referred to
in subsection 14.23(1) in a place that is well ventilated so that the
vapours from the fuel will be dissipated quickly.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Ropes, Slings and Chains]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895523 .Subheading}

- **[[14.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.42
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall, with respect to
  the use and maintenance of any rope, sling or chain or any attachment
  or fitting thereon used by an employee, adopt and implement the
  recommendations set out in Chapter 10 of the Accident Prevention
  Manual for Business and Industry, 10th edition, published by the
  National Safety Council of the United States, dated 1992.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to ropes, slings or
  chains or to any attachments or fittings thereon that comply with the
  requirements of the [Tackle
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1494).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Safe Working Loads]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895529 .Subheading}

- **[[14.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Motorized or manual materials
  handling equipment shall be legibly marked with sufficient information
  so as to enable the operator to determine its safe working load.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No motorized or manual materials handling equipment
  shall be used with a load that exceeds its safe working load.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

#### [Aisles and Corridors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895535 .Subheading}

- **[[14.44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.44
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall provide a clearly
  marked pathway for the exclusive use of pedestrians and persons using
  wheelchairs and other similar devices that is not less than 750 mm
  wide along one side of an aisle, corridor or other course of travel
  that is found in a work place and that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a principal traffic route for mobile equipment,
    pedestrians and persons using wheelchairs and other similar devices;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exceeds 15 m in length.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply where a signaller or
  traffic lights are provided for the purpose of controlling traffic and
  protecting persons.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an aisle, corridor or other course of travel
  that is a principal traffic route in a work place intersects with
  another route, an employer shall cause warning signs marked with the
  words "DANGEROUS INTERSECTION --- CROISEMENT DANGEREUX", in letters
  not less than 50 mm in height on a contrasting background, to be
  posted along the approaches to the intersection.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} At blind corners, mirrors shall be installed that
  permit a mobile equipment operator to see a pedestrian, a person using
  a wheelchair or other similar device, a vehicle or mobile equipment
  approaching the blind corner.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/96-525, s. 15
:::

#### [Clearances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-895545 .Subheading}

- **[[14.45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.45
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In any passageway that is regularly
  travelled by motorized or manual materials handling equipment, the
  employer shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an overhead clearance is at least 150 mm above

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that part of the materials handling equipment or
      its load that is the highest when the materials handling equipment
      is in its highest normal operating position at the point of
      clearance, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the top of the head of the operator or any other
      employee required to ride on the materials handling equipment when
      occupying the highest normal position for the operator or employee
      at the point of clearance; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a side clearance is sufficiently wide to permit the
    motorized or manual materials handling equipment and its load to be
    manoeuvred safely by an operator, but in no case less than 150 mm on
    each side measured from the furthest projecting part of the
    equipment or its load, when the equipment is being operated in a
    normal manner.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an overhead clearance measured in accordance
  with subparagraph (l)(a)(i) or (ii) is less than 300 mm, the employer
  shall cause

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the top of the doorway or object that restricts the
    clearance to be marked with a distinguishing colour or mark; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the height of the passageway in metres to be shown
    near the top of the passageway in letters that are not less than 50
    mm in height and are on a contrasting background.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subparagraph (l)(a)(i) and subsection (2) do not
  apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} motorized materials handling equipment whose course
    of travel is controlled by fixed rails or guides;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that portion of the route of any motorized or
    manual materials handling equipment that is inside a railway car,
    truck or trailer truck, including the warehouse doorway leading
    directly to it; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a load the nature of which precludes compliance
    with that subparagraph or subsection if precautions are taken to
    prevent contact with objects that may restrict the movement of the
    equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 68(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

### [DIVISION III]{.HLabel2}[Manual Handling of Materials]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895561 .Subheading}

- **[[14.46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.46
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, because of the weight, size,
  shape, toxicity or other characteristic of materials, goods or things,
  the manual handling of them may be hazardous to the health or safety
  of an employee, the employer shall issue instructions that the
  materials, goods or things shall, if feasible, not be handled
  manually.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the employer
  shall take into account the frequency and duration of manual lifting
  and the distances and terrain over which an object is to be manually
  lifted or carried in deciding whether the manual handling of the
  materials, goods or things may be hazardous to the health or safety of
  an employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, ss. 29(E), 39
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 106](#1177619-1188184){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177619-1188184.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.46-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.46"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[14.47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.47 .sectionLabel}** No employer shall
require an employee who is an office worker and whose primary tasks do
not include manual lifting or carrying to manually lift or carry
materials, goods or things in excess of 23 kg.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
:::

**[[14.48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.48 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee
is required manually to lift or carry loads weighing in excess of 10 kg,
the employer shall instruct and train the employee

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a safe method of lifting and carrying the loads
  that will minimize the stress on the body; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a work procedure appropriate to the employee's
  physical condition and the conditions of the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 107(F)](#1177621-1188186){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177621-1188186.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.48-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.48"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[14.49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.49 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee
is required manually to lift or carry loads weighing in excess of 45 kg,
the employer shall give instructions to the employee in accordance with
section 14.48 that are

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out in writing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} readily available to the employee; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} kept by the employer for a period of two years after
  they cease to apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 69(F)
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 108(F)](#1177623-1188187){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177623-1188187.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.49-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.49"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION IV]{.HLabel2}[Storage of Materials]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895581 .Subheading}

- **[[14.50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.50
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All materials, goods and things
  shall be stored in the work place in such a manner that the maximum
  safe load carrying capacity of the floor or other supporting
  structures is not exceeded.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All materials, goods or things shall be stored and
  placed in such a manner that employees are not required manually to
  lift materials, goods or things in a manner that would lead to
  overextension of or excessive strain on the body.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} All materials, goods or things shall be stored in a
  manner so that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} light in the storage area is not reduced below the
    levels required by Part VI;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is no obstruction or encroachment of
    passageways, traffic lanes or exits;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the safe operation of motorized or manual materials
    handling equipment is not impeded;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the ready access to or the operation of fire
    fighting equipment is not obstructed;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the operation of fixed fire protection equipment is
    not interfered with; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} there is no risk to the health or safety of any
    employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 39
:::

### [DIVISION V]{.HLabel2}[Transitional Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895594 .Subheading}

- **[[14.51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.51
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), motorized
  materials handling equipment that is in use at the time this amendment
  comes into force, and that meets the requirements of this Part as it
  read immediately before the coming into force of this amendment, is
  exempt from having to comply with the amendment as long as the
  motorized materials handling equipment continues to meet those
  requirements.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), motorized or manual
  materials handling equipment that is in use at the time an amendment
  to a standard or code incorporated by subsection 14.6(1) or 14.8(2) or
  section 14.18 or 14.19 comes into force and that complies with the
  standard or code as it read immediately before the coming into force
  of the amendment, is exempt from having to comply with the amendment
  as long as the motorized or manual materials handling equipment
  continues to meet that standard or code.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall ensure that, if feasible, equipment
  referred to in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subsection (1) meets the requirements of subsection
    14.3(3) or 14.6(2), section 14.10, 14.11 or 14.13 or paragraph
    14.16(1)(b); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsection (2) complies with an amendment referred
    to in that subsection.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible for materials handling
  equipment in use at the time an amendment referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2) comes into force to comply with the amendment,
  the employer whose employees use the equipment shall notify the work
  place committee or the health and safety representative of the
  non-compliance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-400, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 30
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 109](#1177625-1188188){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177625-1188188.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.51-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.51"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART XV]{.HLabel1}[Hazardous Occurrence Investigation, Recording and Reporting]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895604 .Part}

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895605 .Part}

**[[15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[disabling injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[disabling injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employment injury
    or an occupational illness that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents an employee from reporting for work or
      from effectively performing all the duties connected with the
      employee's regular work on any day subsequent to the day on which
      the injury or illness occurred, whether or not that subsequent day
      is a working day for that employee,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} results in the loss by an employee of a body
      member or part thereof or in the complete loss of the usefulness
      of a body member or part thereof, or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} results in the permanent impairment of a body
      function of an employee; ([blessure invalidante]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[district office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[district office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a work place,
    means the office of the Department of Employment and Social
    Development that is responsible for the HRSDC Labour Program and
    that is

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} closest to the work place, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any of the Department's administrative regions
      in which the work place is situated; ([bureau de
      district]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[minor injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[minor injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employment injury or
    an occupational illness for which medical treatment is provided and
    excludes a disabling injury. ([blessure légère]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/2009-147, s. 9
- 2013, c. 40, s. 237
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 8(E)](#1520566-1524902){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520566-1524902.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.1-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 15.1"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895617 .Part}

- **[[15.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), this Part
  does not apply in respect of employees employed in a coal mine or in
  an underground portion of any other type of mine.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 15.10 applies in respect of employees
  employed in a coal mine.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} This Part does not apply in respect of occurrences of
  harassment and violence in the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/90-180, s. 3
- [SOR/2020-130, s. 39](#1251027-1282768){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1251027-1282768.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.2-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.2"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Reports by Employee]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895623 .Part}

**[[15.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.3 .sectionLabel}** If an employee
becomes aware of an accident or other occurrence arising in the course
of or in connection with the employee's work that has caused or is
likely to cause injury to that employee or to any other person, the
employee shall, without delay, report the accident or other occurrence
to the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 110](#1177630-1188191){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177630-1188191.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.3"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Investigations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895627 .Part}

- **[[15.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer becomes aware of
  an accident, occupational illness or other hazardous occurrence
  affecting any of his employees in the course of employment, the
  employer shall, without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appoint a qualified person to carry out an
    investigation of the hazardous occurrence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify the work place committee or the health and
    safety representative of the hazardous occurrence and of the name of
    the person appointed to investigate it; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} take necessary measures to prevent a recurrence of
    the hazardous occurrence.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the hazardous occurrence referred to in
  subsection (1) is an accident involving a motor vehicle on a public
  road that is investigated by a police authority, the investigation
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) shall be carried out by obtaining from
  the appropriate police authority a copy of its report respecting the
  accident.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} As soon as feasible after receipt of the report
  referred to in subsection (2), the employer shall provide a copy of
  the report to the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/94-263, s. 56
- SOR/2002-208, s. 31
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 111](#1177632-1188193){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177632-1188193.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 8(E)](#1520566-1524903){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520566-1524903.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.4-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 15.4"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Telephone or Fax Reports]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895637 .Part}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 112

\]
:::

**[[15.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.5 .sectionLabel}** The employer shall
report to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement, by telephone or fax,
the date, time, location and nature of any accident, occupational
illness or other hazardous occurrence referred to in section 15.4 that
had one of the following results, as soon as feasible but not later than
24 hours after becoming aware of that result:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the death of an employee;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a disabling injury to two or more employees;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the loss by an employee of a body member or a part
  thereof or the complete loss of the usefulness of a body member or a
  part thereof;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the permanent impairment of a body function of an
  employee;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} an explosion;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} damage to a boiler or pressure vessel that results in
  fire or the rupture of the boiler or pressure vessel; or

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} any damage to an elevating device that renders it
  unserviceable, or a free fall of an elevating device.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/94-263, s. 57
- SOR/2002-208, s. 38
- SOR/2014-148, s. 8
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 113](#1177636-1188198){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177636-1188198.html"}
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312231){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312231.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 8(E)](#1520566-1524904){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520566-1524904.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.5-20210701.html "Link to previous version of section 15.5"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Records]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895648 .Part}

- **[[15.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall, within 72 hours
  after a hazardous occurrence referred to in paragraph 15.5(f) or (g),
  record in writing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the hazardous occurrence and the
    date, time and location of the occurrence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the causes of the occurrence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the corrective measures taken or the reason for not
    taking corrective measures.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall, without delay, submit a copy of
  the record referred to in subsection (1) to the work place committee
  or the health and safety representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/94-263, s. 58
- SOR/2002-208, s. 32
:::

## [Minor Injury Records]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895657 .Part}

- **[[15.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall keep a record
  of each minor injury of which the employer is aware that is sustained
  by an employee in the course of employment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record kept pursuant to subsection (1) shall
  contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the occurrence that
    resulted in the minor injury;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the employee affected;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the minor injury; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the causes of the minor injury.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 114(E)](#1177638-1188200){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177638-1188200.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.7"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Written Reports]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895667 .Part}

- **[[15.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall make a report in
  writing, without delay, in the form set out in Schedule I to this Part
  setting out the information required by that form, including the
  results of the investigation referred to in paragraph 15.4(1)(a),
  where that investigation discloses that the hazardous occurrence
  resulted in any one of the following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a disabling injury to an employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an electric shock, toxic atmosphere or oxygen
    deficient atmosphere that caused an employee to lose consciousness;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of rescue, revival or other
    similar emergency procedures; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a fire or an explosion.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall submit a copy of the report
  referred to in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} without delay, to the work place committee or the
    health and safety representative; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within 14 days after the hazardous occurrence, to
    the Head of Compliance and Enforcement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/94-263, s. 59
- SOR/2002-208, s. 33
- SOR/2014-148, s. 9
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 115(F)](#1177640-1188202){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177640-1188202.html"}
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312233){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312233.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.8-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 15.8"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[15.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.9 .sectionLabel}** If an accident
referred to in subsection 15.4(2) results in a circumstance referred to
in subsection 15.8(1), the employer shall, within 14 days after the
receipt of the police report of the accident, submit a copy of that
report to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 38
- SOR/2014-148, s. 10
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312235){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312235.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.9-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 15.9"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Annual Report]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895682 .Part}

- **[[15.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall, not later than
  March 1 in each year, submit to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement
  a written report setting out the number of accidents, occupational
  illnesses and other hazardous occurrences of which the employer is
  aware affecting any employee in the course of employment during the 12
  month period ending on December 31 of the preceding year.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report shall be in the form set out in Schedule
  II to this Part, contain the information required by that form and be
  accompanied by a copy of any report made in accordance with subsection
  19.8(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/2005-401, s. 1
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312237){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312237.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 8(E)](#1520566-1524905){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520566-1524905.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.10-20210701.html "Link to previous version of section 15.10"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Retention of Reports and Records]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895688 .Part}

**[[15.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.11 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall keep a copy of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} each report submitted under section 15.9 or
  subsection 15.10(1) for a period of 10 years following the submission
  of the report to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the record or report referred to in subsection
  15.6(1), 15.7(1) or 15.8(1) for a period of 10 years following the
  hazardous occurrence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-479, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 38
- SOR/2014-148, s. 11
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 6](#1295135-1312239){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295135-1312239.html"}
:::

Version](section-15.11-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 15.11"){.PITLink}
