## [PART XVII]{.HLabel1}[Safe Occupancy of the Work Place]{.HTitleText1} {#h-895931 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895932 .Subheading}

**[[17.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[emergency evacuation plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a written plan
for use in an emergency, prepared in accordance with section 17.4.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895934 .Subheading}

**[[17.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply in respect of employees employed in the underground workings of
mines.

### [Fire Protection Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895936 .Subheading}

- **[[17.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Fire protection equipment shall be
  installed, inspected and maintained in every building in which there
  is a work place in accordance with the standards set out in Parts 6
  and 7 of the National Fire Code.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2000-374, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} All fire protection equipment shall be maintained and
  repaired by a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2000-374, s. 6
:::

### [Emergency Evacuation Plan]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895943 .Subheading}

- **[[17.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where more than 50 employees are
  working in a building at any time, the employer or employers of those
  employees shall prepare an emergency evacuation plan for all
  employees, including those who require special assistance, after
  consultation with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the work place committee or the health and safety
    representative; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employers of any persons working in the
    building to whom the Act does not apply.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An emergency evacuation plan referred to in
  subsection (1) shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a plan of the building, showing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the name, if any, and the address of the
      building,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the owner of the
      building,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the names and locations of the tenants of the
      building,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the date of preparation of the plan,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the scale of the plan,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the location of the building in relation to
      nearby streets and in relation to all buildings and other
      structures located within 30 m of the building,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the maximum number of persons normally
      occupying the building at any time,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} a horizontal projection of the building,
      showing thereon its principal dimensions, and

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} the number of floors above and below ground
      level;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a plan of each floor of the building, showing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the name, if any, and the address of the
      building,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date of preparation of the plan,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the scale of the plan,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a horizontal projection of the floor, showing
      thereon its principal dimensions,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the number of the floor to which the plan
      applies,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the maximum number of persons normally occupying
      the floor at any time,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the location of all fire escapes, fire exits,
      stairways, elevating devices, main corridors and other means of
      exit,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} the location of all fire protection equipment,
      and

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} the location of the main electric power switches
      for the lighting system, elevating devices, principal heating,
      ventilation and air-conditioning equipment and other electrical
      equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a full description of the evacuation procedures to
    be followed in evacuating the building, with the time required to
    complete the evacuation, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} activating the fire alarm,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} notifying the fire department, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} evacuating employees who require special
      assistance; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the names, room numbers and telephone numbers of
    the chief emergency warden and the deputy chief emergency warden of
    the building appointed by the employer or employers under section
    17.7.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An emergency evacuation plan referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be kept up-to-date and shall take into account
  any changes in the building or the nature of its occupancy.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employer referred to in subsection (1) shall keep
  a copy of the up-to-date emergency evacuation plan in the building to
  which it refers.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-263, s. 62
- SOR/96-525, s. 17
- SOR/2002-208, s. 36
:::

### [Emergency Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895978 .Subheading}

- **[[17.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall, after
  consultation with the work place committee or the health and safety
  representative and with the employers of any persons working in the
  building to whom the Act does not apply, prepare emergency procedures

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to be implemented if any person commits or
    threatens to commit an act, other than an occurrence of harassment
    and violence, that may be hazardous to the health and safety of the
    employer or any of their employees;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there is a possibility of an accumulation, spill
    or leak of a hazardous substance in a work place controlled by the
    employer, to be implemented in the event of such an accumulation,
    spill or leak;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if more than 50 employees are working in a building
    at any time, to be implemented when evacuation is not an appropriate
    means of ensuring the health and safety of employees;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to be implemented in the event of a failure of the
    lighting system; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to be implemented in the event of a fire.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The emergency procedures referred to in
  subsection (1) shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an emergency evacuation plan, where applicable, or
    a plan for evacuating employees who require special assistance to be
    implemented in the event of a fire;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a full description of the procedures to be
    followed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the location of the emergency equipment provided by
    the employer; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a plan of the building, showing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the name, if any, and the address of the
      building, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the owner of the
      building.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The plan for the evacuation of employees who require
  special assistance shall be established in consultation with those
  employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-68, s. 14
- SOR/94-263, s. 63
- SOR/96-525, s. 18
- SOR/2002-208, s. 37
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 126](#1177668-1188227){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177668-1188227.html"}
- [SOR/2020-130, s. 40](#1251029-1282770){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1251029-1282770.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.5-20190625.html "Link to previous version of section 17.5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Instructions and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-895996 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 127(F)

\]
:::

- **[[17.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee shall be instructed
  and trained in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to be followed by him in the event
    of an emergency; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the location, use and operation of fire protection
    equipment and emergency equipment provided by the employer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notices that set out the details of the evacuation
  plans and procedures referred to in paragraphs 17.4(2)(c) and
  17.5(2)(a) and (b) shall be posted at locations accessible to every
  employee at the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-525, s. 19
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 128(F)](#1177672-1188234){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177672-1188234.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.6-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.6"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Emergency Wardens]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896004 .Subheading}

- **[[17.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer or employers have
  prepared an emergency evacuation plan for a building, the employer or
  employers shall appoint

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a chief emergency warden and a deputy chief
    emergency warden for that building;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an emergency warden and a deputy emergency warden
    for each floor of the building that is occupied by employees of the
    employer or employers; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} monitors for any employee who require special
    assistance in evacuating the building.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The chief emergency warden and deputy chief emergency
  warden appointed for a building shall be employees who are normally
  employed in the building.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The emergency warden and the deputy emergency warden
  appointed for a floor in a building shall be employees who are
  normally employed on that floor and monitors for an employee who
  requires special assistance shall be employees who are normally
  employed on the same floor as the employee requiring special
  assistance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/96-525, s. 20
:::

- **[[17.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every emergency warden, deputy
  emergency warden and monitor appointed under section 17.7 shall be
  instructed and trained in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} his responsibilities under the emergency evacuation
    plan and the emergency procedures referred to in paragraph
    17.5(1)(c); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the use of fire protection equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record of all instruction and training provided in
  accordance with subsection (1) shall be kept by the employer in the
  work place to which it applies for a period of two years from the date
  on which the instruction or training is provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 76(E)
- SOR/96-525, s. 21
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 129(F)](#1177674-1188236){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177674-1188236.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.8"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Inspections]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896021 .Subheading}

- **[[17.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to the inspections carried out under section 17.3, a visual
  inspection of every building to which subsection 17.4(1) applies shall
  be carried out by a qualified person at least once every six months
  and shall include an inspection of all fire escapes, exits, stairways
  and fire protection equipment in the building in order to ensure that
  they are in serviceable condition and ready for use at all times.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record of each inspection carried out in accordance
  with subsection (1) shall be dated and signed by the person who made
  the inspection and kept by the employer in the building to which it
  applies for a period of two years from the date on which it is signed.

### [Meetings of Emergency Wardens and Drills]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896025 .Subheading}

- **[[17.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At least once every year and after
  any change is made in the emergency evacuation plan or the emergency
  procedures referred to in paragraph 17.5(1)(c) for a building,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} emergency wardens, deputy emergency wardens and
    monitors appointed under section 17.7 and employees requiring
    special assistance shall meet for the purpose of ensuring that they
    are familiar with the emergency evacuation plan and the emergency
    procedures and their responsibilities thereunder; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an evacuation or emergency drill shall be conducted
    for the employees in that building.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer or employers shall keep a record of each
  meeting and drill referred to in subsection (1) in the building
  referred to in that subsection for a period of two years from the date
  of the meeting or the drill.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (2) shall
  contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each meeting,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of the meeting,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the names and titles of those present, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the matters discussed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each drill,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date and time of the drill, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where applicable, the length of time taken to
      evacuate the building.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall notify the local fire department
  for the building where an evacuation or emergency drill is to take
  place at least 24 hours in advance of the date and time of the drill.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 77(E)
- SOR/96-525, s. 22
:::

### [Fire Hazard Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896042 .Subheading}

- **[[17.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no person
  shall, in a fire hazard area,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} use any equipment, machinery or tool of a type that
    may provide a source of ignition; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} smoke or use an open flame or other source of
    ignition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not feasible to avoid performing work
  involving the use of any equipment, machinery or tool that may provide
  a source of ignition in an area that has an atmosphere that contains
  or is likely to contain explosive concentrations of combustible dust
  or in an area where combustible dust has accumulated in a sufficient
  quantity to be a fire hazard, the following shall apply:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the atmosphere and surfaces in the area where the
    work is to be performed and within that portion of the surrounding
    area that is accessible to sparks or pieces of hot metal produced by
    the work shall be substantially free of combustible dust;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where any equipment, machinery or tool produces
    combustible dust that may reach the areas referred to in paragraph
    (a), the equipment, machinery or tool shall be made inoperative
    prior to and during the time the work is being performed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if feasible, the area where the work is to be
    performed shall be enclosed to prevent the escape of sparks or
    pieces of hot metal produced by the work;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all openings in floors and walls through which
    sparks or pieces of hot metal produced by the work may pass shall be
    sealed or covered to prevent such passage;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any combustible materials within the areas referred
    to in paragraph (a) shall be removed or, if it is not feasible,
    shall be covered with a non-combustible protective covering;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} floors and walls of combustible material within the
    areas referred to in paragraph (a) shall be protected from the fire
    hazard by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} drenching the surfaces of the floors and walls
      with water, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} covering the floors and walls with a
      non-combustible protective covering;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the work shall be performed under the supervision
    of a qualified person, who shall remain in the work area while the
    work is performed and for 30 minutes thereafter; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} there shall be readily available in the work area
    at least one hand-held portable fire extinguisher and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a water hose at least 25 mm in diameter that is
      connected to a water supply line, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a supply of not less than 200 L of water and a
      bucket.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-632, s. 78(F)
- SOR/94-263, s. 64
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 130](#1177679-1188237){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177679-1188237.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.11"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[17.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.12 .sectionLabel}** Signs shall be
posted in conspicuous places at all entrances to a fire hazard area

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} identifying the area as a fire hazard area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting the use of an open flame or other source
  of ignition in the area.

## [PART XVIII]{.HLabel1}[Diving Operations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-896065 .Part}

### [Definitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896066 .Subheading}

**[[18.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.1 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in
this section apply in this Part.

[[contaminated environment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[contaminated environment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a point of discharge of effluent from a sewer, a
      water or sewage treatment plant or an industrial plant;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a site where chemical or biological effluent has
      accumulated; or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the site of an oil or radioactive spill.
      ([environnement contaminé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[decompression table]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[decompression table]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a table or set of
    tables that shows a schedule of rates for the safe ascent of a diver
    from depth in order to minimize the risk of decompression sickness.
    ([table de décompression]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dive supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dive supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a qualified person who
    has been designated by the employer to be in charge of a diving
    operation at the dive site, including the health and safety of its
    divers. ([chef de plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a qualified person who performs
    work under water. ([plongeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diver's flag]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diver's flag]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the rectangular red flag
    set out in Schedule I, each side of which is not less than 50 cm in
    length, that has a white diagonal stripe extending from the tip of
    the hoist to the bottom of the flag. ([pavillon du
    plongeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diver's tender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diver's tender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a qualified person who
    attends to a diver for the duration of a dive. ([assistant du
    plongeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hyperbaric chamber]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hyperbaric chamber]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a pressure vessel
    and associated equipment designed to subject humans to
    greater-than-atmospheric pressures. ([caisson
    hyperbare]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[International Code Flag A]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[International Code Flag A]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the white
    and dark blue flag set out in Schedule II, which is not less than 1
    m in height. ([pavillon A du code international]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[liveboating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[liveboating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the support of a diving
    operation from a vessel that is not at anchor, made fast to the
    shore or a fixed structure, or aground. ([plongée avec
    bateau-soutien]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[no-decompression limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[no-decompression limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the maximum
    time that can be spent at a depth without requiring a decompression
    stop. ([limite de remontée sans palier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[surface supply dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[surface supply dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving operation
    where a diver is supplied with breathing mixtures by a life support
    umbilical from the surface. ([plongée non autonome]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[therapeutic recompression]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[therapeutic recompression]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    treatment of a diver in a hyperbaric chamber in accordance with
    generally accepted tables and practices. ([recompression
    thérapeutique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[type 1 dives]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[type 1 dives]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means diving operations

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the primary purpose of which is

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to conduct scientific, archaeological or other
        research operations, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to gather evidence or information relating to
        a crime;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} do not require decompression,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} do not involve diving in the vicinity of
        underwater pressure differentials,

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} are not related to the search, construction,
        repair or inspection of ships, bridge piers, wharves, dry docks,
        underwater tunnels, or water control and water intake
        facilities, and

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} do not involve using underwater welding or
        cutting equipment; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the depth of which does not exceed 40 m.
      ([plongées de type 1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[type 2 dives]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[type 2 dives]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means diving operations other
    than type 1 dives. ([plongées de type 2]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 39
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896096 .Subheading}

**[[18.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to diving operations to which the [Canada Oil and Gas Diving
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-600), the [Nova Scotia Offshore
Area Petroleum Diving Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-95-189) or the
[Newfoundland Offshore Area Petroleum Diving
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-601) apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Division I applies to type 1 dives
  and type 2 dives.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Division II applies to type 2 dives.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[Type 1 Dives and Type 2 Dives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896105 .Subheading}

#### [Written Procedures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896106 .Subheading}

- **[[18.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall establish
  written procedures and requirements that are to be followed and met by
  employees involved in diving operations and that specify which of
  those procedures and requirements

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} apply to each type of dive in which the employees
    are likely to engage, including the qualifications of the dive team
    members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are to be implemented or met to counter any known
    hazards, including those arising out of contaminated or potentially
    contaminated environments, low visibility, hazardous water flow
    conditions and entrapment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} deal with emergency situations and the evacuation
    of dive team members.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall review the procedures referred
  to in paragraph (1)(c) at least once a year and, if necessary, revise
  those procedures.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The procedures and requirements referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be available to all employees involved in diving
  operations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Instruction and Training]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896116 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 131(F)

\]
:::

- **[[18.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that an
  employee required to dive has received instruction and training with
  respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the types of dives in which the employee is likely
    to participate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the equipment that the employee is likely to use.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that an employee involved
  in diving operations demonstrates on an annual basis that the employee
  is competent to perform the types of dives in which the employee is
  likely to participate.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that persons who are not
  employees and who dive with employees demonstrate that they are
  competent to perform the types of dives in which they will
  participate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 132(F)](#1177694-1188244){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177694-1188244.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.5"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[18.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that an
  employee required to dive has been trained in first aid and
  cardio-pulmonary resuscitation and in the recognition of the symptoms
  and the management of diving-related injuries.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where oxygen equipment for therapeutic purposes is
  provided at the dive site, the employer shall ensure that an employee
  required to dive or act as a diver's tender is trained in its use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Medical Evaluations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896130 .Subheading}

- **[[18.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that an
  employee required to dive has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} received a medical examination within the last two
    years; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} been declared fit to dive, or fit to dive with
    specified restrictions, by the examining physician.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the examining
  physician shall use as a guideline the factors and tests listed in
  Appendices A and B of CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z275.2-92, Occupational
  Safety Code for Diving Operations, published in English in April 1992
  and in French in February 1994, as amended from time to time.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the physician examining the employee pursuant
  to subsection (1) declares the employee fit to dive with specified
  restrictions, the employer shall not permit the employee to dive
  otherwise than in accordance with the specified restrictions.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that a diver who has been
  treated for a pressure-related injury or illness does not dive unless
  written clearance for further diving is given by a physician.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344408){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344408.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.7"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fitness to Dive]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896140 .Subheading}

- **[[18.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a diver considers himself or herself unfit to dive owing to illness,
  fatigue or any other cause, the diver shall inform the employer of
  that fact.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer who has been notified pursuant to
  subsection (1) shall not permit the employee to dive.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 7(F)](#1520564-1524895){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520564-1524895.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.8"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Dive Plan]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896146 .Subheading}

- **[[18.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that,
  for each dive, the dive team develops a dive plan that identifies the
  surface and underwater conditions and hazards likely to be
  encountered, including those arising from contaminated environments
  and underwater pressure differentials, and that specifies

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the duties of each dive team member;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the diving equipment to be used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the breathing supply requirements, including the
    reserve supply;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the thermal protection to be used;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the repetitive dive factor;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the no-decompression limit;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the emergency procedures to be followed;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the communication methods to be used;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a type 1 dive that is a scuba dive, whether
    there is a need for a diver's tender;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which the dive must be
    terminated;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to be followed to ensure that
    machinery, equipment or devices that could create a hazard have been
    locked out; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} whether lifelines must be used.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a type 1 dive, the dive plan referred
  to in subsection (1) shall also specify whether there is a need for a
  standby diver.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a type 2 dive that requires
  decompression, the dive plan referred to in subsection (1) shall also
  specify the decompression schedule to be used.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 133(F)](#1177696-1188245){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177696-1188245.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.9"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Dive Team]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896165 .Subheading}

- **[[18.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4)
  and Division II, every employer shall ensure that a dive team
  consisting of at least two divers is present at every dive site.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} One member of the dive team referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be designated as the dive supervisor.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A diver's tender shall be present at any surface
  supply dive.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A dive boat operator shall be present at any dive
  carried out from a boat or vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.11 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that, for the duration of a surface supply dive, the
diver's tender devotes his or her entire time and attention to the work
of a diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.12 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that, for the duration of a type 2 dive carried out from a
boat or vessel, the dive boat operator devotes his or her entire time
and attention to the work as a dive boat operator.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.13 .sectionLabel}** A standby diver
shall be present at all times when type 2 dives are in progress.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.14 .sectionLabel}** Where a standby
diver is needed pursuant to subsection 18.9(2) or section 18.13, the
standby diver shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be trained and equipped to operate at the depth at
  which and in the circumstances in which a submerged diver is
  operating;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} be readily available to assist the submerged diver in
  the event of an emergency; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} not dive or be required to dive except in an
  emergency.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Emergency Assistance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896188 .Subheading}

**[[18.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.15 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall, for the purposes of paragraph 18.9(1)(g), arrange for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} assistance in the event of an emergency;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} medical support on a 24-hour-a-day basis and a
  suitable means of communication between the dive site and that medical
  support; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} evacuation of a diver to a hyperbaric chamber if
  necessary.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Pressure-related Injury]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896195 .Subheading}

**[[18.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.16 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that, when a diver shows any indication of a
pressure-related injury or requires therapeutic recompression,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the necessary first-aid treatment is initiated; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the medical support referred to in paragraph 18.15(b)
  is notified immediately.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Decompression]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896201 .Subheading}

**[[18.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.17 .sectionLabel}** Diving operations,
repetitive dives and the treatment of divers shall be carried out in
accordance with generally accepted decompression tables and procedures.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Identification of Dive Site]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896205 .Subheading}

**[[18.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.18 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that the following flags are conspicuously displayed at or
in close proximity to the dive site whenever diving operations are
conducted in areas of marine traffic:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the International Code Flag A, hoisted from any
  vessel, boat or platform used in support of a dive in such a manner as
  to ensure all-round visibility of the dive site; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one or more diver's flags from a white buoy which may
  be equipped with

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a light, in which case the light shall be yellow
    and flashing, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} reflecting material, in which case the reflecting
    material shall be yellow.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Supervision]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896213 .Subheading}

**[[18.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.19 .sectionLabel}** Every diving
operation shall be conducted under the supervision of a dive supervisor.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.20 .sectionLabel}** The primary duties
of the dive supervisor shall include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensuring that every member of the dive team is
  familiar with the dive plan;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensuring that every member of the dive team
  understands the member's duties, including the emergency procedures to
  be followed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensuring, before each dive, that all necessary
  equipment is available and in good operating condition; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} supervising the entire diving operation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Hazards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896224 .Subheading}

**[[18.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.21 .sectionLabel}** Immediately before
each dive, the dive supervisor shall review the nature of any hazards at
the dive site and ensure that all divers are fully aware of the hazards
likely to be encountered in the diving operation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 134(F)](#1177698-1188246){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177698-1188246.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.21-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.21"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Communications]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896228 .Subheading}

**[[18.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.22 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall, for the purposes of paragraph 18.9(1)(h), ensure that all means
of communication used at the dive site are

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} appropriate for the operation; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} understood by all members of the dive team.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Breathing Supply]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896234 .Subheading}

- **[[18.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that a
  reserve breathing supply sufficient to allow the safe termination of a
  dive is immediately available to the diver.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where required by the dive plan, the reserve
  breathing supply referred to in subsection (1) shall be carried by the
  diver.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.24 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that air compressors and filter systems are tested annually
and produce breathing mixtures that meet the requirements of clause 3.8
of CSA Standard CAN/CSA-Z275.2-92, Occupational Safety Code for Diving
Operations, published in English in April 1992 and in French in February
1994, as amended from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344409){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344409.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.24-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.24"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[18.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.25 .sectionLabel}** Where a dive plan
requires that oxygen for therapeutic purposes be available, the employer
shall supply

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} demand-type oxygen therapy equipment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an adequate quantity of oxygen.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Diving Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896248 .Subheading}

- **[[18.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that all
  diving equipment used by employees is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designed for its intended use and maintained in a
    condition that ensures its continuing operation for the purpose and
    at the depth for which it was designed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} inspected, tested, maintained and calibrated by a
    qualified person at intervals recommended by the manufacturer and
    whenever the equipment is thought to be defective.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that diving equipment
  used by persons who are not employees and who are granted access to
  the work place is in a condition that ensures its operation for the
  purpose and at the depth for which it was designed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Immediately before each dive, every
  diver shall check that all the equipment the diver requires is
  present, properly fastened in place and functioning.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before beginning a descent, every diver shall conduct
  the check specified in subsection (1) again in the water.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that
  whenever diving operations are carried out from a diving station
  located more than 2 m above the water, the divers are transported
  through the air-water interface by a cage, basket or platform.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that a stationary
  platform from which a diver works, or any cage, basket or platform on
  or in which a diver is lowered to or raised from an underwater work
  place, and any associated hoisting devices and tackle,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are used for the purpose for which they were
    designed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} do not in themselves create a hazard.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any cage, basket or platform and any associated
  equipment referred to in subsection (2) shall be dedicated to the
  diving operations until the dive is completed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.29 .sectionLabel}** Floating equipment
used in diving operations, including a vessel that is anchored or
moored, shall not be moved or relocated while a diver is in the water
unless the dive supervisor agrees to the move or relocation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.30 .sectionLabel}** The employer shall
ensure that, when a floating platform, vessel or boat is used in support
of the dive, it remains on site at all times while a diver is in the
water.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that,
  where a dive plan requires the use of a lifeline to tether a diver,
  the lifeline

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is free of knots and splices, other than knots and
    splices necessary to attach the lifeline to the diver and the dive
    site;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has a breaking strength of not less than 1400 kg;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is secured to the diver so as to prevent loss of
    contact with the diver; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is secured at the surface to a safe point of
    anchorage.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A lifeline shall always be used in dives taking place
  under ice.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that a lifeline is tended
  at all times by a diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.32 .sectionLabel}** Every submersible
pressure gauge and every depth gauge shall be inspected by a qualified
person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} before being used for the first time;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} thereafter at intervals not exceeding 12 months; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} whenever the gauge is thought to be defective.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee finds a defect in
  any diving equipment, including pressure gauges and depth gauges, that
  may render it unsafe for use, the employee shall immediately report
  the defect to the employer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall mark or tag as unsafe and remove
  from service any diving equipment, including pressure gauges and depth
  gauges, that may be used by employees where a defect may render it
  unsafe for use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Termination of Dive]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896296 .Subheading}

**[[18.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.34 .sectionLabel}** A dive shall be
terminated in accordance with the dive plan referred to in subsection
18.9(1) or when

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a dive team member requests termination;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a diver loses contact with or fails to respond
  correctly to a communication from a diving partner;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a diver loses contact with or fails to respond
  correctly to a communication from the diver's tender;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a diver's primary breathing supply fails; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a diver becomes aware of any sign of a malfunction of
  equipment or any sign or symptom of diver distress.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Observation After Diving]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896305 .Subheading}

**[[18.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.35 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that, on completion of a dive, a diver remains under
observation for a period of time sufficient to ensure the health and
safety of the diver.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- SOR/2002-208, s. 41
:::

#### [Flying After Diving]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896309 .Subheading}

- **[[18.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall not allow a diver
  to fly at an altitude greater than 300 m above the altitude of the
  dive site unless the following period of time has elapsed:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 12 hours following a no-decompression dive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 24 hours following a decompression dive; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} such time as is specified by a physician who
    treated the diver for a pressure-related injury.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an emergency air
  evacuation.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the event of an emergency air evacuation,
  provision shall be made to furnish the diver with oxygen, and the
  flight altitude and in-flight conditions shall be those recommended by
  the attending physician or dive supervisor.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Reports and Records]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896319 .Subheading}

- **[[18.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every diver shall report to the
  employer any occurrence that has caused a diving-related injury to the
  diver.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall investigate the occurrence
  reported pursuant to subsection (1) and keep a written record of its
  findings.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 135(F)](#1177700-1188247){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177700-1188247.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.37-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.37"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[18.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that a
  dive record is kept for every diver.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (1) shall
  contain, for each dive,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of the dive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the dive location;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of the diver;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name of the standby diver, if any;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the diver's tender, if any;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the signature of the diver and the dive supervisor;
    and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the breathing mixture used, if other than air.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (1) shall also
  contain, for each type 1 dive,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total elapsed time, measured in minutes, from
    the time the diver leaves the surface to the time the diver begins
    final ascent, rounded to the next whole minute;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum depth reached; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any unusual incident or condition, including
    emergency decompression time.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (1) shall also
  contain, for each type 2 dive,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the type of diving equipment used;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the time the diver leaves the surface;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the maximum depth reached;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the time the diver begins final ascent;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the time the diver reaches the surface;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the decompression schedule used, if any; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any unusual incident or condition.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep the record referred to in
  subsection (1) for a period of 12 months after the date of the dive.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 136](#1177702-1188248){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177702-1188248.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.38-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.38"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[18.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.39
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall keep a dated
  dive record for each diver that shall include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the year in which the dive occurs;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum depth reached;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the total elapsed time, measured in minutes, from
    the time the diver leaves the surface to the time the diver begins
    final ascent, rounded to the next whole minute;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the breathing mixture used, if other than air;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any unusual incident or condition;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any occurrence reported pursuant to subsection
    18.37(1); and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a copy of any record referred to in subsection
    18.37(2).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall annually supply the record
  referred to in subsection (1) to the diver and keep a copy for a
  period of five years after the date on which the diver ceases to be
  employed by the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 137(F)](#1177704-1188250){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177704-1188250.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.39-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.39"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[18.40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.40 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall keep a record of all diver instruction and training received and
all competency demonstrations given pursuant to section 18.5, for as
long as the employee is employed by the employer as a diver.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 138(F)](#1177706-1188251){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177706-1188251.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.40-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.40"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[18.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.41 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall keep a record of each air quality test performed in accordance
with section 18.24 for a period of five years after the day on which the
test was made.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 139(E)](#1177708-1188252){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177708-1188252.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.41-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.41"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[18.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.42 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall keep a record of each equipment inspection, test, maintenance and
calibration performed in accordance with paragraph 18.26(1)(b) for a
period of five years after the day on which the inspection, test,
maintenance or calibration was performed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 139(E)](#1177708-1188254){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177708-1188254.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.42-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.42"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Type 2 Dives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896371 .Subheading}

#### [Approach to Water Control and Intake Facilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896372 .Subheading}

**[[18.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.43 .sectionLabel}** Underwater
approaches to water control and intake facilities where underwater
pressure differentials may be encountered shall be made in accordance
with sections 18.44 to 18.46.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 140(F)](#1177710-1188256){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177710-1188256.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.43-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.43"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[18.44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.44 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that a diver working near a facility referred to in section
18.43 wears a lifeline tended from a position outside the approach area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.45 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that any diver required to approach an underwater intake
pipe, tunnel or duct is provided with the means to differentiate the
intake from any other similar intake in the dive area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.46
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  employer shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a diver is not allowed to approach any underwater
    intake or structure where underwater pressure differentials may be
    encountered until the flow of water is stopped or controlled; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the flow of water is not re-established until the
    diver leaves the water or until the dive supervisor has determined
    that the diver is clear of the approach area referred to in
    paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the flow of water referred to in subsection (1)
  cannot be stopped, the employer shall assess the safety of a diver
  approaching the intake by determining flow patterns using reliable
  indicators, direct measurements or calculations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 141(F)](#1177712-1188257){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177712-1188257.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.46-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.46"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Diving Hazards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896389 .Subheading}

**[[18.47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.47 .sectionLabel}** No employer shall
permit a diver to approach a work place that may be hazardous because of
the operation of machinery or equipment, unless the machinery or
equipment is secured against inadvertent movement and made inoperable
for the duration of the dive.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.48 .sectionLabel}** If there is a risk
that a diver could be entrapped, the employer shall ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a two-way voice communication system between the
  diver and the diver's tender is provided; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a second dive team, equipped to rescue a diver in the
  event of an emergency, is present at the dive site.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 142(E)](#1177717-1188259){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177717-1188259.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.48-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.48"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Use of Explosives]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896398 .Subheading}

- **[[18.49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.49
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The initiation of underwater
  explosive charges at a dive site shall be under the direct control of
  the dive supervisor.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A two-way voice communication system shall be
  provided when explosives are being used at a dive site, unless the
  voice communication system itself would pose a hazard.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Hyperbaric Chambers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896404 .Subheading}

**[[18.50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.50 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that a hyperbaric chamber meeting the requirements of CSA
Standard CAN/CSA Z275.1-93, Hyperbaric Facilities, published in English
in December 1993 and in French in January 1995, as amended from time to
time, for Class A (double-lock type) hyperbaric chambers, is available
and in operable condition whenever

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a decompression dive is occurring; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the depth of a dive is to exceed 40 m.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 5(F)](#1343801-1344410){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343801-1344410.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.50-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.50"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[18.51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.51 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that a hyperbaric chamber is operated by a qualified
person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Alternative Energy Sources]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896413 .Subheading}

- **[[18.52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.52
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that a
  second source of power capable of supplying sufficient power to
  operate all essential diving equipment is available in the event of
  failure of the primary power source.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall ensure that the second source of
  power referred to in subsection (1) is capable of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} being rapidly brought on line; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} operating all equipment essential to the diving
    operation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 143(F)](#1177719-1188260){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177719-1188260.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.52-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.52"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Surface Supply Type 2 Dives]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896421 .Subheading}

**[[18.53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.53 .sectionLabel}** Sections 18.54 to
18.62 apply to surface supply type 2 dives.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.54 .sectionLabel}** Where the planned
depth of a dive does not exceed 40 m, there shall be at least three
persons present at the dive site, of whom

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least two are divers, one of whom is a standby
  diver; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least one is a diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.55 .sectionLabel}** The diver's tender
or the standby diver of a diving operation, the depth of which does not
exceed 40 m, shall be designated as the dive supervisor.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.56 .sectionLabel}** Where the planned
depth of a dive exceeds 40 m, there shall be at least four persons at
the dive site, of whom

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least two are divers, one of whom is a standby
  diver;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one is the dive supervisor; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} one is the diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.57 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that, except in an emergency, each surface supply diver in
the water has a separate diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.58
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The voice communication system
  provided between a diver and the surface shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} allow the diver's breathing to be heard at the
    surface; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include a recording system where the maximum depth
    of the dive is greater than 55 m.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An emergency signal system must be in effect during a
  diving operation to supplement the primary communication system.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.59 .sectionLabel}** A reserve breathing
supply appropriate for the dive shall be carried by each diver.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.60 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that nonreturn valves are

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} fitted to all diving helmets and surface supply
  diving masks; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} checked daily before the commencement of diving
  operations in accordance with the manufacturer's recommendations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.61 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that every life support umbilical incorporates a lifeline
rigged to prevent stress on the air line.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.62 .sectionLabel}** Every employer
shall ensure that, in a liveboating operation,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a method that will prevent the lifeline or life
  support umbilical from becoming entangled in the propellers is used;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the diver's tender is a qualified person for the type
  of tending used; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel operator is a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

#### [Type 2 Scuba Diving]{.HTitleText3} {#h-896466 .Subheading}

**[[18.63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.63 .sectionLabel}** Sections 18.64 to
18.67 apply to type 2 dives in the course of which divers use scuba.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.64 .sectionLabel}** In a diving
operation where the diver is tethered to the surface by a lifeline or
float, there shall be at least three persons present at the dive site,
of whom

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one is a standby diver; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one is a diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.65 .sectionLabel}** In a diving
operation where the diver is not tethered to the surface by a lifeline
or float, there shall be a through-water, two-way voice communication
system between the divers and between the divers and the surface, and at
least four persons present at the dive site, of whom

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} three are divers, one of whom is a standby diver; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one is a diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

**[[18.66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.66 .sectionLabel}** One of the
employees on the surface shall be designated as the dive supervisor of
the scuba diving operation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

- **[[18.67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.67
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), dives
  shall be limited to a depth not exceeding 40 m.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A diver may dive to a depth greater than 40 m for the
  purpose of saving a life provided that, where conditions permit, the
  diver is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} secured by a lifeline; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} tended by a diver's tender.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-456, s. 1
:::

## [PART XIX]{.HLabel1}[Hazard Prevention Program]{.HTitleText1} {#h-896504 .Part}

### [Hazard Prevention Program]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896505 .Subheading}

- **[[19.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall, in consultation
  with and with the participation of the policy committee, or, if there
  is no policy committee, the work place committee or the health and
  safety representative, develop, implement and monitor a program for
  the prevention of hazards, including ergonomics-related hazards, in
  the work place that is appropriate to the size of the work place and
  the nature of the hazards and that includes the following components:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an implementation plan;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hazard identification and assessment methodology;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} hazard identification and assessment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} preventive measures;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} employee training; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a program evaluation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2009-84, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2007-271, s. 1
- SOR/2009-84, s. 2
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 144(E)](#1177721-1188261){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177721-1188261.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.1-20090305.html "Link to previous version of section 19.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Implementation Plan]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896517 .Subheading}

- **[[19.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} develop an implementation plan that specifies the
    time frame for each phase of the development and implementation of
    the prevention program;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} monitor the progress of the implementation of the
    preventive measures; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} review the time frame of the implementation plan
    regularly and, as necessary, revise it.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In implementing the prevention program, the employer
  shall ensure that ergonomics-related hazards are identified and
  assessed and that they are eliminated or reduced, if feasible, as
  required by subsection 19.5(1) and that any person assigned to
  identify and assess ergonomics-related hazards has the necessary
  instructions and training.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2007-271, s. 2
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 145](#1177723-1188263){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177723-1188263.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.2-20071129.html "Link to previous version of section 19.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Hazard Identification and Assessment Methodology]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896526 .Subheading}

- **[[19.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall develop a hazard
  identification and assessment methodology, including an identification
  and assessment methodology for ergonomics-related hazards, taking into
  account the following documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any hazardous occurrence investigation reports;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} first aid records and minor injury records;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} work place health protection programs;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any results of work place inspections;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any employee reports made under paragraph 126(1)(g)
    or (h) of the Act or under section 15.3;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any government or employer reports, studies and
    tests concerning the health and safety of employees;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any reports made under the [Safety and Health
    Committees and Representatives
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-305);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the record of hazardous substances; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information, including
    ergonomics-related information.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The hazard identification and assessment methodology
  shall include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the steps and time frame for identifying and
    assessing the hazards;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the keeping of a record of the hazards; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a time frame for reviewing and, if necessary,
    revising the methodology.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2007-271, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 19.3"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Hazard Identification and Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896544 .Subheading}

**[[19.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.4 .sectionLabel}** The employer shall
identify and assess the hazards in the work place, including
ergonomics-related hazards, in accordance with the methodology developed
under section 19.3 taking into account

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the hazard;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} in the case of ergonomics-related hazards, all
  ergonomics-related factors such as

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the physical demands of the work activities, the
    work environment, the work procedures, the organization of the work
    and the circumstances in which the work activities are performed,
    and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the characteristics of materials, goods, persons,
    animals, things and work spaces and the features of tools and
    equipment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employees' level of exposure to the hazard;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the frequency and duration of employees' exposure to
  the hazard;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the effects, real or apprehended, of the exposure on
  the health and safety of employees;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the preventive measures in place to address the
  hazard;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} any employee reports made under paragraph 126(1)(g)
  or (h) of the Act or under section 15.3; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2007-271, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 19.4"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Preventive Measures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896558 .Subheading}

- **[[19.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall, in order to
  address identified and assessed hazards, including ergonomics-related
  hazards, take preventive measures to address the assessed hazard in
  the following order of priority:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the elimination of the hazard, including by way of
    engineering controls which may involve mechanical aids, equipment
    design or redesign that take into account the physical attributes of
    the employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reduction of the hazard, including isolating
    it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the provision of personal protective equipment,
    clothing, devices or materials; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} administrative procedures, such as the management
    of hazard exposure and recovery periods and the management of work
    patterns and methods.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} As part of the preventive measures, the employer
  shall develop and implement a preventive maintenance program in order
  to avoid failures that could result in a hazard to employees.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall ensure that any preventive measure
  shall not in itself create a hazard and shall take into account the
  effects on the work place.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The preventive measures shall include steps to
  address

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} newly identified hazards in an expeditious manner;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ergonomics-related hazards that are identified when
    planning implementation of change to the work environment or to work
    duties, equipment, practices or processes.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall ensure that any person assigned to
  implement ergonomics-related prevention measures has the necessary
  instruction and training.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2007-271, s. 5
- SOR/2009-84, s. 3(F)
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 146(F)](#1177725-1188265){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177725-1188265.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.5-20090305.html "Link to previous version of section 19.5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Employee Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896573 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2019-246, s. 147(E)

\]
:::

- **[[19.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall provide each
  employee with health and safety training, including training relating
  to ergonomics, which shall include the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the hazard prevention program implemented in
    accordance with this Part to prevent hazards applicable to the
    employee, including the hazard identification and assessment
    methodology and the preventive measures taken by the employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the work place and the hazards
    associated with it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee's duty to report under paragraphs
    126(1)(g) and (h) of the Act and under section 15.3; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an overview of the Act and these Regulations.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall provide training to an employee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whenever new hazard information in respect of a
    hazard in the work place becomes available to the employer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shortly before the employee is assigned a new
    activity or exposed to a new hazard.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall review the employee training
  program, and, if necessary, revise it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least every three years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whenever there is a change in conditions in respect
    of the hazards; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whenever new hazard information in respect of a
    hazard in the work place becomes available to the employer.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Each time training is provided to an employee, the
  employee shall acknowledge in writing that they received it and the
  employer shall acknowledge in writing that they provided it.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep a paper or electronic record
  of the training provided to each employee for a period of two years
  after the employee ceases to be exposed to a hazard.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2007-271, s. 6
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 148](#1177729-1188269){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177729-1188269.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.6-20071129.html "Link to previous version of section 19.6"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Program Evaluation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896591 .Subheading}

- **[[19.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall evaluate the
  effectiveness of the hazard prevention program, including its
  ergonomics-related components, and, if necessary, revise it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least every three years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whenever there is a change in conditions in respect
    of the hazards; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whenever new hazard information in respect of a
    hazard in the work place becomes available to the employer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The evaluation of the effectiveness of the prevention
  program shall be based on the following documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conditions related to the work place and the
    activities of the employees;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any work place inspection reports;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any hazardous occurrence investigation reports;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any safety audits;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} first aid records and any injury statistics,
    including records and statistics relating to ergonomics-related
    first aid and injuries;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any observations of the policy and work place
    committees, or the health and safety representative, on the
    effectiveness of the prevention program; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant information.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2007-271, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 19.7"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Reports]{.HTitleText2} {#h-896607 .Subheading}

- **[[19.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a program evaluation has been conducted under section 19.7, the
  employer shall prepare a program evaluation report.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep readily available every
  program evaluation report for six years after the date of the report.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-401, s. 2
- SOR/2009-84, s. 4
- [SOR/2019-246, s. 149(F)](#1177740-1188275){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1177740-1188275.html"}
:::

:::

## [PART XX]{.HLabel1}[Regular Rate of Wages]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1395583 .Part}

- **[[20.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 146.5 of
  the Act, the regular rate of wages of an employee who is paid on a
  basis other than an hourly rate is calculated or determined in
  accordance with this section.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4) to (6), if the employee
  worked for at least one hour during the four-week period preceding a
  week in which the employee attends the appeal proceeding as a party or
  in response to a summons by the Board, the employee's regular rate of
  wages for that week is calculated by dividing the wages that the
  employee earned in that period by the hours that they worked during
  the same period, excluding overtime hours.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4) to (6), if the employee
  did not work for at least one hour in the period referred to in
  subsection (2) but did work for at least one hour in the preceding
  four-week period, their regular rate of wages is calculated using the
  formula set out in subsection (2) but in respect of the preceding
  four-week period.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (5) and (6), if the employee
  is paid, in whole or in part, on a commission basis and the employee
  has completed at least 12 weeks of continuous employment for their
  employer, the employee's regular rate of wages for the week in which
  the employee attends the appeal proceeding as a party or in response
  to a summons by the Board is calculated by dividing the amount of
  wages that they earned in the 12-week period preceding that week by
  the number of hours that the employee worked during that period,
  excluding overtime hours.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), if a collective agreement
  that is binding on the employee and employer sets out a regular rate
  of wages that is applicable to the employee, or a method for
  calculating it, that rate, or the rate calculated following that
  method, is the employee's regular rate of wages.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} In the following circumstances, an employee's regular
  rate of wages is the minimum wage rate referred to in Part III of the
  Act:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee's regular rate of wages cannot be
    calculated or determined in accordance with any of subsections (2)
    to (5) because the employer is not required, under paragraph
    24(2)(d) of the [Canada Labour Standards
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._986), to keep records of
    hours worked by the employee each day and the employer cannot
    otherwise determine the number of hours that the employee worked
    during the applicable period; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee's regular rate of wages, as calculated
    or determined in accordance with any of subsections (2) to (5), is
    less than that minimum wage rate.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (2) to (4), vacation
  pay, general holiday pay, personal leave pay, pay for leave for
  victims of family violence, bereavement leave pay, overtime pay and
  pay received under section 146.5, subsection 205(2) or 251.12(5) or
  section 288 of the Act are not taken into account in the calculation
  of wages earned.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
  [[week]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period between midnight on
  Saturday and midnight on the immediately following Saturday.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-65, s. 5](#1395119-1395596){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1395119-1395596.html"}
:::

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- SOR/2016-141, s. 77

  - [77]{.lawlabel} If an employer complies with the requirements set
    out in the provisions amended by these Regulations as those
    provisions read immediately before February 11, 2015, the amendments
    made by these Regulations do not apply to the employer

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} during the period that begins on the day on which
      these Regulations come into force and ends on November 30, 2018;
      and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any hazardous products that are in
      the work place on December 1, 2018, during the period that begins
      on December 1, 2018 and ends on May 31, 2019.

- --- SOR/2020-130, s. 42

  - [42]{.lawlabel} Sections 20.1, 20.2 and 20.9 of the [Canada
    Occupational Health and Safety
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-304), as they read immediately
    before the day on which the [Work Place Harassment and Violence
    Prevention Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-130) come into
    force, continue to apply to all "work place violence", as described
    in section 20.2 of the [Canada Occupational Health and Safety
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-304) and alleged work place
    violence, of which the employer becomes aware before the day on
    which the [Work Place Harassment and Violence Prevention
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-130) come into force.
